is sufficiently large, guaranteeing statistically optimal estimation. Part (2) extends the analysis to settings where exploration rates are small, possibly decreasing or even vanishing, and establishes that long-term statistically optimal rates are still attainable for arms in
{\mathcal{A}}
, while the suboptimal arms maintain uniformly bounded estimation error. This result extends existing literature on estimation error and exploration schedules; a detailed comparison is provided in Section
4.4
. The following corollary provides a sharper characterization when
\delta_{t}=C\log t
.

Corollary 2

(Estimation Error Dynamics —
\delta_{t}=C\log t
)
.

Under the same conditions and the same
t_{1}
and
\{\eta_{t}\}
as in Theorem
4
, we have:

1.

If
\pi_{t}\geq\pi
and
C_{a}\geq 2K/\pi
,
C_{b}\geq CC_{a}^{2}\lambda_{\max}^{2}/\lambda_{\min}^{2}
, then with probability at least
1-CKC_{a}e^{-cd}
,
\|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{i}^{(t)}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{i}^{*}\|^{2}\leq\frac{C^{*}}{\lambda_{\min}}\frac{d+\log t}{t+C_{b}d}\sigma^{2}
for all
t
.

2.

For any
\pi_{t}\in[0,1]
and
t\geq t_{1}
, with probability at least
1-|{\mathcal{A}}|e^{-cd}-|{\mathcal{A}}^{c}|e^{-cd}\cdot\sum_{l=t_{1}}^{t+1}{\mathbb{I}}\left\{\pi_{l}\neq 0\right\}
, we have for all
i\in{\mathcal{A}}
,
\|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{i}^{(t)}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{i}^{*}\|^{2}\leq\frac{C^{**}}{\lambda_{\min}}\frac{d+\log t}{t+C_{b}d}\sigma^{2}
, and for all
i\in{\mathcal{A}}^{c}
,
\|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{i}^{(t)}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{i}^{*}\|^{2}\leq\frac{h^{2}}{4}
.

Corollary
2
(1)
implies that if
\pi_{t}\geq\pi
, then for all
T\leq+\infty
, the estimator satisfies, with probability at least
1-CKC_{b}\exp(-cd)
,

\displaystyle\|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{i}^{(T)}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{i}^{*}\|^{2}\leq C^{*}\frac{\max\{d,\log T\}}{\lambda_{\min}(T+C_{b}d)}\sigma^{2},\quad T=0,1,\cdots,+\infty.

(15)

If
T\geq\exp(d)
, the extra
\log T
term becomes unavoidable, as shown in Section
2
. Corollary
2
(2)
further covers settings where the exploration rate may be arbitrarily small. Under appropriate conditions, e.g., where
\pi_{t}=0
for all
t\geq t_{2}
or
{\mathcal{A}}^{c}=\emptyset
, the estimator for arms
i\in{\mathcal{A}}
satisfies the same bound for all
T\leq\infty
.

Before presenting the regret bound, we introduce the following measures of model complexity:

\displaystyle\textsf{CoM}_{2}:=\sum_{j=1}^{K}\sum_{i=1}^{K}\|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{i}^{*}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{j}^{*}\|,\quad\textsf{CoM}_{1}:=\sum_{j=1}^{K}\max_{i}\|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{i}^{*}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{j}^{*}\|,\quad\textsf{CoM}_{\infty}:=\max_{i,j}\|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{i}^{*}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{j}^{*}\|.

When the pairwise distances among the parameters
\{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{i}^{*}\}
are large, the parameter
h
in Assumption
5
can also be chosen large, potentially simplifying the identification of optimal arms. However, incorrect decisions in such settings can incur larger regret. The next theorem formalizes how these complexity measures influence regret. Define the accumulated tail probability

\textsf{Tail}(T,d,\{\delta_{l}\}):=e^{-cd}\bigg(c^{\prime}C_{b}+K\sum_{l=0}^{t_{1}}e^{-c\delta_{l}}+|{\mathcal{A}}|\sum_{l=t_{1}+1}^{T}e^{-c\delta_{l}}+|{\mathcal{A}}^{c}|\sum_{l=t_{1}+1}^{T}{\mathbb{I}}\{\pi_{l}\neq 0\}\bigg).

Theorem 5

(Regret Bound under General Learning Rates)
.

Under the same assumptions, stepsize choices, and the same
t_{1}
and parameter conditions as in Theorem
4
, suppose
\pi_{t}\geq 1/3
for
t\leq t_{1}
and
\pi_{t}\in[0,1]
for
t\geq t_{1}
. Then

\textsf{Regret}(T)\leq\underbrace{\sqrt{\lambda_{\max}}\frac{\sum_{t=0}^{T}\pi_{t}}{K}\min\{\textsf{CoM}_{2},\sqrt{d}\textsf{CoM}_{1}\}}_{R_{1}}\\
+\underbrace{\sqrt{\lambda_{\max}}\textsf{Tail}(T,d,\{\delta_{t}\})\min\{\textsf{CoM}_{2},\sqrt{d}\textsf{CoM}_{\infty}\}}_{R_{2}}\\
+\underbrace{C\sqrt{\frac{\lambda_{\max}}{\lambda_{\min}}}\sigma\min\{K,\sqrt{d}\}\sum_{t=0}^{T}\sqrt{\frac{d+\delta_{t}}{t+C_{b}d}}}_{R_{3}}.

Theorem
5
decomposes the cumulative regret into three components. The first term,
R_{1}
, captures regret due to exploration and scales with
\sum_{t=0}^{T}\pi_{t}
, which represents the approximate number of exploration iterations. The second term,
R_{2}
, arises from the failure probability in Theorem
4
and is typically negligible. The third term,
R_{3}
, captures the dominant contribution and scales as
\sqrt{T}
. To minimize overall regret, one should therefore keep
\sum_{t=0}^{T}\pi_{t}
as small as possible while still satisfying the required conditions. As discussed in Theorem
4
, a convenient choice is to set
\pi_{t}=f(t)
for some
f
in the class
\Pi(\tau,\pi)
.

Definition 3
.

A function
f(\cdot):{\mathbb{R}}^{+}\to{\mathbb{R}}^{+}
belongs to
\Pi(\tau,\pi)
if
(i)

f
is decreasing;
(ii)

0\leq f(x)\leq 1
; and
(iii)

f(x)\geq\pi
for all
x\leq\tau
.

Since
f
is decreasing, we have
\sum_{t=1}^{T}\pi_{t}\leq\int_{0}^{T}f(x){\rm d}x
. The following are some examples of the exploration rate
\pi_{t}
that satisfy Definition
3
.

Example 4

(Example Functions satisfying Definition
3
)
.

The following functions belong to
\Pi(\tau,1/3)
.

1.

Let
f(x)=\frac{C_{\pi}}{x+2C_{\pi}}
, where
C_{\pi}\geq\tau
. Then
f\in\Pi(\tau,1/3)
and
\sum_{t=1}^{T}\pi_{t}\leq C_{\pi}\ln\left(\frac{T+2C_{\pi}}{T}\right)
;

2.

Let
f(x)=(\frac{C_{\pi}}{x+2^{1/p}C_{\pi}})^{p}
with
0<p<1
and
C_{\pi}\geq 3^{1-1/p}\tau
. Then
f\in\Pi(\tau,1/3)
and
\sum_{t=1}^{T}\pi_{t}\leq C_{\pi}^{p}(T+2^{1/p}C_{\pi})^{1-p}
.

Substituting
\delta_{t}=0
or
\delta_{t}=\log t
into Theorem
5
gives the following corollary.

Corollary 3

(Regret: Unaware of
h
)
.

Assume the same assumptions and parameters as in Theorem
5
. Let
\eta_{t}=\frac{1}{\lambda_{\min}}\frac{C_{a}}{t+C_{b}d}
and
\pi_{t}=f(t)\in\Pi(t_{1},1/3)
. If
{\mathcal{A}}^{c}\neq\emptyset
, then for all
T\leq\exp(Cd)
,

\textsf{Regret}(T)\leq\sqrt{\lambda_{\max}}\frac{\int_{0}^{T}f(t){\rm d}t}{K}\min\{\textsf{CoM}_{2},\sqrt{d}\textsf{CoM}_{1}\}+C\sqrt{C^{*}}\sqrt{\frac{\lambda_{\max}}{\lambda_{\min}}}\frac{\sigma\min\{K,\sqrt{d}\}T\sqrt{d}}{\sqrt{T+C_{b}d}+\sqrt{C_{b}d}}.

If
{\mathcal{A}}^{c}=\emptyset
, then for all
T\leq+\infty
,

\textsf{Regret}(T)\leq\sqrt{\lambda_{\max}}\frac{\int_{0}^{T}f(t){\rm d}t}{K}\min\{\textsf{CoM}_{2},\sqrt{d}\textsf{CoM}_{1}\}+C\sqrt{C^{*}}\sqrt{\frac{\lambda_{\max}}{\lambda_{\min}}}\frac{\sigma\min\{K,\sqrt{d}\}T\sqrt{d+\log T}}{\sqrt{T+C_{b}d}+\sqrt{C_{b}d}}.

The cumulative regret bound consists of two main components. The first depends on the horizon
T
via the integral
\int_{0}^{T}f(s){\rm d}s
, determined by the exploration schedule and potentially growing more slowly than
\sqrt{T}
. The second term scales as
\sqrt{T}
. For instance, choosing
\pi_{t}=(\frac{C_{\pi}}{t+2^{1/p}C_{\pi}})^{p}
with
1/2\leq p\leq 1
(Example
4
) yields

\textsf{Regret}(T)\leq O(\sqrt{T}).

Thus, Theorems
4
and
5
together show that optimal regret and estimation error can be achieved simultaneously under a wide class of exploration schedules. Moreover, the dependence on
T
and
d
is statistically optimal. In some settings, e.g., as discussed in
5
, the parameter
h
may be treated as known. When
h
is known, Theorems
4
and
5
imply that exploration is unnecessary once the estimation error satisfies
\max_{i\in[K]}\|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{i}^{(t)}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{i}^{*}\|\leq h/2
. Thus, the exploration rate may be set to
\pi_{t}=0
beyond this point, as formalized below.

Corollary 4

(Regret: Aware of
h
)
.

Under the same assumptions and conditions as Theorem
5
, take
\eta_{t}=\frac{1}{\lambda_{\min}}\frac{C_{a}}{t+C_{b}d}
and set
\pi_{t}\geq 1/2
for
t<t_{1}
and
\pi_{t}=0
for
t\geq t_{1}
. Then for all
T\leq\infty
,

\textsf{Regret}(T)\leq\sqrt{\lambda_{\max}}\frac{t_{1}}{K}\min\{\textsf{CoM}_{2},\sqrt{d}\textsf{CoM}_{1}\}\\
+C\sqrt{C^{*}}\sqrt{\frac{\lambda_{\max}}{\lambda_{\min}}}\frac{\sigma\min\{K,\sqrt{d}\}T\sqrt{d+\log T}}{\sqrt{T+C_{b}d}+\sqrt{C_{b}d}}.

Setting
\pi_{t}=0
ensures that suboptimal arms in
{\mathcal{A}}^{c}
are never selected. Compared with Corollary
3
, this result removes the restriction
T\leq C\exp(cd)
even when
{\mathcal{A}}^{c}\neq\emptyset
. The improvement stems from the fact that with
\pi_{t}=0
, exploration-induced errors no longer accumulate, allowing non-asymptotic guarantees to extend to an infinite horizon.

4.3
Asymptotic Analysis and Inference

In this section, we investigate the asymptotic behavior of the iterates
{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{i}^{(t)}
, from which we derive confidence intervals for
{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{i}^{*}
. There is a growing literature on inference for SGD iterates in contextual linear bandits; see, for example,
12
,
31
,
14
, and
21
, which study inference under i.i.d. covariates and noise with
1/t^{\alpha}
stepsize schemes, constant exploration rates, and related settings. A detailed comparison with these approaches is provided in Section
4.4
.

Throughout, we maintain the assumptions
{\mathbb{E}}\{\xi_{t}|{\mathcal{F}}_{t}^{+}\}=0
and
{\mathbb{E}}\{{\mathbf{X}}_{t}|{\mathcal{F}}_{t}\}=\boldsymbol{0}
almost surely. To ensure asymptotic convergence of the SGD iterates, we require the existence of limiting second-moment matrices for both the covariates and the noise.

Assumption 6

(Covariates and Noise for Asymptotic Convergence)
.

There exist matrices
{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{*}
and
{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{i}^{*}
such that

\displaystyle\lim_{t\to+\infty}{\mathbb{E}}\big\{\|{\mathbb{E}}\{{\mathbf{X}}_{t}^{\top}{\mathbf{X}}_{t}|{\mathcal{F}}_{t}\}-{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{*}\|\big\}=0~~\mbox{ and }~~\displaystyle\lim_{t\to+\infty}{\mathbb{E}}\big\{\|{\mathbb{E}}\{{\mathbf{X}}_{t}{\mathbf{X}}_{t}^{\top}\cdot{\mathbb{I}}\{{\mathbf{X}}_{t}\in{\mathcal{U}}_{i}^{*}\}|{\mathcal{F}}_{t}\}-{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{i}^{*}\|\big\}=0.

In addition, there exist constants
\lambda_{\max},\lambda_{\min}>0
such that almost surely,

\lambda_{\min}{\mathbf{I}}\preceq{\mathbb{E}}\{{\mathbf{X}}_{t}{\mathbf{X}}_{t}^{\top}|{\mathcal{F}}_{t}\}~~\mbox{ and }~~\displaystyle\sup_{{\mathbf{V}}\in{\mathbb{S}}^{d-1}:\,{\mathbf{V}}\in{\mathcal{F}}_{t}}{\mathbb{E}}\{({\mathbf{X}}_{t}^{\top}{\mathbf{V}})^{4}|{\mathcal{F}}_{t}\}\leq\lambda_{\max}^{2}.

Assume
\xi_{t}\perp{\mathbf{X}}_{t}|{\mathcal{F}}_{t}
. There exist
\sigma,\sigma_{*}>0
such that
\displaystyle\lim_{t\to+\infty}{\mathbb{E}}\{|{\mathbb{E}}\{\xi_{t}^{2}|{\mathcal{F}}_{t}\}-\sigma_{*}^{2}|\}=0
and
{\mathbb{E}}\{\xi_{t}^{4}|{\mathcal{F}}_{t}^{+}\}\leq\sigma^{4}
almost surely.

Assumption
6
is needed to establish the asymptotic distribution of
{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{i}^{(t)}
. However, near the boundary
\partial{\mathcal{U}}_{i}^{*}
, the behavior
{\mathbf{X}}_{t}
may still cause difficulties. To illustrate, consider the case
{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{1}^{*}=(3,0)^{\top}
and
{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{2}^{*}=(-1,0)^{\top}
, and suppose

{\mathbb{P}}\{{\mathbf{X}}=(0,1)^{\top}\}={\mathbb{P}}\{{\mathbf{X}}=(0,-1)^{\top}\}=\frac{1}{2}{\mathbb{P}}\{{\mathbf{X}}=(2,0)^{\top}\}={\mathbb{P}}\{{\mathbf{X}}=(-1,0)^{\top}\}=\frac{1}{5}.

Here,
\partial{\mathcal{U}}_{1}^{*}=\{(0,y)^{\top}:y\in{\mathbb{R}}\}
is the
y
-axis, but
{\mathbb{P}}({\mathbf{X}}\in\partial{\mathcal{U}}_{1}^{*})=2/5
. Although our non-asymptotic guarantees in Theorems
4
and
5
continue to hold, the asymptotic behavior depends on sensitivity on the frequency with which
{\mathbf{X}}_{t}
lies near the decision boundary. In particular, Assumption
6
alone is insufficient. To address this, we introduce spherical measures that quantify how
{\mathbf{X}}_{t}
spreads on the unit sphere. For any measurable
A\in\sigma({\mathbb{S}}^{d-1})
, define

\nu_{X}(A;t):={\mathbb{E}}\big(\|{\mathbf{X}}_{t}\|\cdot{\mathbb{I}}\{{\mathbf{X}}_{t}/\|{\mathbf{X}}_{t}\|\in A\}\big|{\mathcal{F}}_{t}\big),\quad\kappa_{X}(A;t):={\mathbb{E}}\big(\|{\mathbf{X}}_{t}\|^{2}\cdot{\mathbb{I}}\{{\mathbf{X}}_{t}/\|{\mathbf{X}}_{t}\|\in A\}\big|{\mathcal{F}}_{t}\big).

We impose the following mild regularity condition near
\partial{\mathcal{U}}_{i}^{*}
.

Assumption 7
.

In addition to Assumption
5
, there exists
h_{0}\in(0,h]
and constants
\kappa_{0},\nu_{0}>0
such that for any
h_{1},h_{2}\in[-h_{0},h_{0}]
,
\kappa_{X}({\mathcal{U}}_{i}(h_{1})\backslash{\mathcal{U}}_{i}(h_{2}))\leq\kappa_{0}|h_{1}-h_{2}|
and
\nu_{X}({\mathcal{U}}_{i}(h_{1})\backslash{\mathcal{U}}_{i}(h_{2}))\leq\nu_{0}|h_{1}-h_{2}|
.

Assumption
7
includes the conditions of covariates in
13
;
31
. The following remark provides an explicit example for Assumption
7
.

Remark 6
.

Assumption
7
encompasses a broad class of distributions. For simplicity of presentation, we take
{\mathbf{X}}_{t}
to be i.i.d., though the arguments extend to the dependent case. In this remark, we write
\kappa_{X}(\cdot)
and
\nu_{X}(\cdot)
without the index
t
. Let
\mu
denote the Lebesgue measure on the sphere
{\mathbb{S}}^{d-1}
. By Lebesgue’s Decomposition Theorem, the measure
\nu_{X}
admits the decomposition

\nu_{X}=\nu_{X}^{(1)}+\nu_{X}^{(2)},

where
\nu_{X}^{(1)}\ll\mu
and
\nu_{X}^{(2)}\perp\mu
. Radon–Nikodym’s theorem then guarantees the existence of the derivative
\frac{{\rm d}\nu_{X}^{(1)}}{{\rm d}\mu}
, and for any
A\in\sigma({\mathbb{S}}^{d-1})
,

\nu_{X}^{(1)}(A)=\int_{A}\frac{{\rm d}\nu_{X}^{(1)}}{{\rm d}\mu}{\rm d}\mu.

If
\nu_{X}^{(2)}\bigl({\mathcal{U}}_{i}(-h_{0})\setminus{\mathcal{U}}_{i}(h_{0})\bigr)=0
and
\frac{d\nu_{X}^{(1)}}{d\mu}\leq\kappa_{0}
, then Assumption
7
holds for
\nu_{X}
. The same reasoning applies to
\kappa_{X}
. Common distributions such as multivariate Gaussian and multivariate Student’s
t
distributions fall within the scope of Assumption
7
.

If Assumption
5
and Assumption
7
hold with parameters
h
and
h_{0}
, respectively, then they also hold with any smaller
h^{\prime}<\min\{h,h_{0}\}
. Thus, for simplicity, we assume throughout that Assumptions
5
and
7
hold with a common parameter
h
. We define

\displaystyle{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{i}(\pi^{*}):=\frac{\pi^{*}}{K}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{*}+(1-\pi^{*}){\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{i}^{*},

and write its singular value decomposition as
{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{i}(\pi^{*})={\mathbf{U}}_{i}(\pi^{*})\boldsymbol{\Lambda}_{i}(\pi^{*}){\mathbf{U}}_{i}(\pi^{*})^{\top}
, where
{\mathbf{U}}_{i}(\pi^{*})
is orthogonal and
\boldsymbol{\Lambda}_{i}(\pi^{*})=\operatorname{diag}\{\lambda_{j}(\pi^{*})\}
. Let
C_{a}^{\prime}=C_{a}/\lambda_{\min}
, so that the stepsize is
\eta_{t}=C_{a}^{\prime}/(t+C_{b}d)
. We further define

\boldsymbol{\Lambda}_{i}(\pi^{*},C_{a}^{\prime},\sigma_{*}):=\operatorname{diag}\bigg\{\frac{\sigma_{*}^{2}}{\lambda_{j}(\pi^{*})}\cdot\frac{(C_{a}^{\prime}\lambda_{j}(\pi^{*}))^{2}}{2C_{a}^{\prime}\lambda_{j}(\pi^{*})-1}\bigg\}.

We now present a Bahadur-type representation for the SGD iterates. Note that
\{X_{t}\}
and
\{\xi_{t}\}
form a Markov process rather than an i.i.d. sequence. Throughout,
O_{{\mathbb{P}}}(\cdot)
denotes stochastic boundedness.

Proposition 2

(Bahadur-Type Representation)
.

Suppose Assumptions
5
,
6
, and
7
hold for some common
h
.
2
2

2

We assume a common
h
for clarity; if Assumption
5
holds for
h
, it automatically holds for any smaller
h^{\prime}<h
.
Under the same
\{\eta_{t}\}
and
\{\pi_{t}\}
as in Theorem
5
, if
\lim_{t\to+\infty}\pi_{t}=\pi^{*}
, then

\displaystyle{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{i}^{(t)}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{i}^{*}=\sum_{j=0}^{t}\prod_{l=j+1}^{t}\Big\{{\mathbf{I}}-\eta_{l}\cdot\Big(\frac{\pi_{l}}{K}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}+(1-\pi_{l}){\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{i}^{*}\Big)\Big\}\eta_{j}\cdot\xi_{j}\cdot{\mathbf{X}}_{j}\cdot{\mathbb{I}}\left\{a_{j}=i\right\}+{\mathbf{R}}_{i}^{(t)}.

Moreover, under either of the following conditions:

(1)

\pi^{*}=0
,
i\in{\mathcal{A}}
and on the event
\cup_{t=t_{1}}^{+\infty}\{\max\|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{i}^{(t)}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{i}^{*}\|\leq\frac{h}{2}\}
;

(2)

\pi^{*}>0
,
C_{a}\geq 4K/\pi^{*}
, and
i\in[K]
,

we have
{\mathbf{R}}_{i}^{(t)}=O_{{\mathbb{P}}}(1/t)
and

\displaystyle\sqrt{t}\sum_{j=0}^{t}\prod_{l=j+1}^{t}\Big\{{\mathbf{I}}-\eta_{l}\cdot\Big(\frac{\pi_{l}}{K}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}+(1-\pi_{l}){\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{i}(\pi^{*})\Big)\Big\}\eta_{j}\xi_{j}{\mathbf{X}}_{j}\cdot{\mathbb{I}}\{a_{j}=i\}

\displaystyle~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~\rightsquigarrow N(0,{\mathbf{U}}_{i}(\pi^{*})\boldsymbol{\Lambda}_{i}(\pi^{*},C_{a}^{\prime},\sigma_{*}){\mathbf{U}}_{i}(\pi^{*})^{\top}).

The asymptotic distribution notably does not depend on
C_{b}
. Proposition
2
allows the construction of confidence intervals for
{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{i}^{*}
. Since
\pi^{*}
and
C_{a}^{\prime}
are known, the remaining task is to estimate
{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}
and
{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{i}^{*}
.

Lemma 6

(Estimation of
{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}
and
{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{i}^{*}
)
.

Under Assumption
6
, the estimator

\displaystyle\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}}^{*}(t):=\frac{1}{t}\sum_{l=1}^{t}{\mathbf{X}}_{l}{\mathbf{X}}_{l}^{\top}

converges in probability to
{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{*}
under Frobenius norm.
3
3

3

Any norm equivalent to the Frobenius norm may be used.
Furthermore, if
{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{i}^{(t)}\to{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{i}^{*}
in probability, then

\displaystyle\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}}_{i}(t):=\frac{1}{(1-\pi^{*})t}\sum_{l=1}^{t}{\mathbf{X}}_{l}{\mathbf{X}}_{l}^{\top}\cdot{\mathbb{I}}\{a_{l}=i,\alpha_{l}=0\}\to{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{i}^{*},\quad\widehat{\sigma}_{*}^{2}(t):=\frac{1}{t}\sum_{l=1}^{t}(Y_{l}-{\mathbf{X}}_{l}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{a_{l}})^{2}\to\sigma_{*}^{2},

both in probability.

Since
\pi^{*}
and
C_{a}^{\prime}
are known, we construct

\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}}_{i}(\pi^{*},t):=\frac{\pi^{*}}{K}\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}}^{*}(t)+(1-\pi^{*})\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}}_{i}(t),

and let its singular value decomposition be
\widehat{{\mathbf{U}}}_{i}(\pi^{*},t)\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}_{i}(\pi^{*},t)\widehat{{\mathbf{U}}}_{i}(\pi^{*},t)^{\top}
. By Lemma
6
and and the Davis–Kahan theorem
(
17
;
71
)
,

\displaystyle\widehat{{\mathbf{U}}}_{i}(\pi^{*},t)\to{\mathbf{U}}_{i}(\pi^{*}),\quad\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}_{i}(\pi^{*},t)\to\boldsymbol{\Lambda_{i}}(\pi^{*}),

in probability. Consequently,

\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}_{i}(t):=\operatorname{diag}\bigg\{\frac{\widehat{\sigma}_{*}^{2}(t)}{\widehat{\lambda}_{j}(\pi^{*},t)}\cdot\frac{(C_{a}^{\prime}\widehat{\lambda}_{j}(\pi^{*},t))^{2}}{2C_{a}^{\prime}\widehat{\lambda}_{j}(\pi^{*},t)-1}\bigg\}\to\boldsymbol{\Lambda}_{i}(\pi^{*},C_{a}^{\prime},\sigma_{*})\quad\text{in probability}.

For any set
{\mathcal{C}}\subseteq{\mathbb{R}}^{d}
with probability mass
1-\alpha
under
N(0,{\mathbf{I}})
,

\lim_{t\to\infty}{\mathbb{P}}\big\{\sqrt{t}\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}_{i}(t)^{-1/2}({\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{i}^{(t)}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{i}^{*})\in{\mathcal{C}}\big\}=1-\alpha,

which yields asymptotically valid confidence intervals for
{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{i}^{*}
.

4.4
Related Literature

In this section, we review the literature on linear contextual bandits and related online learning approaches. The pioneering works of
29
,
5
, and
4
estimate the underlying parameters of multi-arm contextual bandits using ordinary least squares (OLS) or LASSO applied to stored historical observations. Their analyses are non-asymptotic and rely on bounded i.i.d. covariates together with i.i.d. noise. These studies impose a margin condition under which the optimal regret rate is
\log(T)
. In contrast, our framework does not require this condition; instead, the minimax-optimal regret rate in our setting is of order
\sqrt{T}
. Additional offline contextual bandit algorithms can be found in
19
,
36
, and
54
.
37
and
13
investigate the asymptotic properties of Markov linear regression, but do not address regret. Both works rely on OLS estimators. In particular,
13
focus on bounded i.i.d. covariates and i.i.d. noise in the special case of two arms (
K=2
). To date, no offline algorithm is known to achieve optimal non-asymptotic performance when covariates and noise follow a Markov process. Our work contributes to this gap by providing both non-asymptotic and asymptotic analysis for this setting in an
online
fashion, without storing historical observations.

More recently,
12
and
14
take computational and memory constraints into account by employing SGD. Their analyses also focus on the two-arm setting (
K=2
), where the suboptimal arm set
{\mathcal{A}}^{c}
is empty. They assume i.i.d. covariates and noise.
14
further study scenarios with a constant exploration rate, which yields
O(T)
regret but enables asymptotically Gaussian inference for the estimator. It is important to emphasize that the general multi-arm setting with
K>2
is
not
a straightforward extension of the two-arm case, either asymptotically or non-asymptotically; see, for example, Proposition
2
. By applying Theorem
4
and Theorem
5
to the two-arm case with i.i.d. covariates and noise, our approach improves upon existing online contextual bandit results, sharpening the regret from
\textsf{Regret}(T)=O(T^{2/3})
to the minimax-optimal
\sqrt{T}
rate. Moreover, we strengthen the Bahadur-type representation by obtaining a residual term that is stochastically
O(1/t)
. Our framework removes the classical exploration-exploitation trade-off by fully leveraging dependent data, enabling the estimation error for arms in
{\mathcal{A}}
to match the performance of fully exploratory designs. In contrast to prior work, which requires the exploration rate not to decay too quickly, our results accommodate a broad range of exploration schemes and even allow
\pi_{t}=0
for sufficiently large
t
; see Theorem
5
, Proposition
2
, and Corollaries
3
and
4
.

Another line of work studies online bandits with low-rank matrix structures
(
31
;
21
;
43
)
. These methods also rely on SGD updates, but use stepsizes of order
1/t^{\alpha}
with
\alpha\in(0,1)
, a strategy different from ours. Their regret bounds typically scale as
T^{2/3}
, which exceeds the minimax lower bound
\sqrt{T}
. Whether the low-rank structure introduces additional technical challenges remains an open question and is an interesting direction for future research.

5
Numeric Experiments

This section presents a series of numerical experiments evaluating the performance of the proposed algorithms. Across all settings, the empirical results align closely with the theoretical predictions. Unless otherwise specified, we set the ambient dimension to
d=99
and the time horizon to
T=999{,}999
.

Linear Regression.

We begin with experiments for online linear regression (Theorem
1
, Section
2
). The procedure employs an initial constant stepsize to stabilize the residual scale, followed by a decaying stepsize of the form
\frac{C_{a}}{t-t_{1}+C_{b}d}
. Figure
2
reports convergence dynamics for several choices of
C_{a}\in\{3,10,50\}
under a common
C_{b}
. The resulting estimation trajectories are remarkably similar across the different stepsize magnitudes. Figures
2(a)
and
2(b)
then compare two data-generating processes: (i) i.i.d. covariates and noise, and (ii) dependent covariates and noise generated according to

\displaystyle{\mathbf{X}}_{t}=\text{Independent Rad(1/2)}\times\frac{1}{\|{\mathbf{X}}_{t-1}\|}\times{\mathbf{X}}_{t-1}+N(\boldsymbol{0},{\mathbf{I}}_{d}),

(16)

\displaystyle\xi_{t}=\text{Independent Rad(1/2)}\times\frac{\operatorname{sign}(\xi_{t-1})\min\{|\xi_{t-1}|,1\}}{|{\mathbf{X}}_{t-1}(1)|}\times{\mathbf{X}}_{t-1}(1)+N(\boldsymbol{0},{\mathbf{I}}_{d}),

(17)

where Rad
(1/2)
denotes Rademacher random variables and
N(\boldsymbol{0},{\mathbf{I}}_{d})
represents standard multivariate Gaussian. Both settings use the same signal-to-noise ratio. Notably, dependence in the data does not impede estimation accuracy, consistent with our theoretical guarantees.

Figure
3
investigates sensitivity with respect to
C_{b}
while fixing
C_{a}
. Even under substantial variations of
C_{b}
, the long-run covariance behavior remains nearly identical. Figure
3(b)
shows that this robustness persists when the covariates and noise follow the dependent processes (
16
)–(
17
), again mirroring the i.i.d. results in Figure
3(a)
.

Sparse Linear Regression.

This segment presents the numerical experiment for online sparse linear regression (Theorem
3
, Section
3
). When the time horizon is sufficiently large, the support of
{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}
can be fully recovered. We set
T=9{,}999{,}999
and
|\operatorname{supp}({\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*})|=4
. Moreover, the covariates and noise are generated to be dependent following Equation
16
and
17
. The dense SGD algorithm with a constant stepsize scheme (Theorem
1
(1)) is used for initialization. After this initialization phase, we perform a one-step hard-thresholding, retaining the six largest entries of
{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t}
in absolute value. We compare the performance of the sparse SGD method (Theorem
3
) with that of the dense-parameter SGD algorithm (Theorem
1
). For a fair comparison, both algorithms use the same tuning parameters,
C_{a}=3
and
C_{b}=100
.

Figure
4(a)
corresponds to a weak signal-to-noise ratio setting,
\|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\|/{\mathbb{E}}|\xi|=5
, where the largest signal equals
4{\mathbb{E}}|\xi|
and the smallest is as low as
0.004{\mathbb{E}}|\xi|
. After initialization, only two nonzero components are recovered. We set
\alpha=7
in Theorem
3
, meaning that the sparse algorithm updates the support of
{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t}
at most seven times. Despite the extremely weak magnitude of the remaining two signals relative to the noise, the sparse algorithm successfully identifies all four support entries by the end of the time horizon. As shown in Figure
4(a)
, the sparse estimator achieves substantially smaller estimation error than the dense SGD estimator. Figure
4(b)
reports the results under a high signal-to-noise ratio. After the constant stepsize phase and the one-step hard-thresholding, three of the four signals are recovered. The sparse algorithm subsequently identifies the final missing signal, again producing markedly smaller estimation error than the dense SGD algorithm.

Linear Bandit.

We next study the contextual linear bandit setting (Theorems
4
and
5
, Section
4
). Following the observations from the linear regression experiments, which show negligible accuracy loss under dependence, we focus here on the dependent data-generating processes (
16
) and (
17
). We set
K=5
arms, of which exactly one is suboptimal, and choose parameters satisfying Assumption
5
. The stepsize schedule mirrors the regression experiment: a short warm-start phase with constant stepsize, followed by
\eta_{t}=\frac{20}{t-t_{1}+50d}
.

Figure
5
compares estimation accuracy and regret across several exploration schemes. Figure
5(a)
reports the estimation error of the optimal arm. Notably, the estimation trajectories under the decaying exploration rates
\pi_{t}=\frac{5}{\sqrt{t-t_{1}+50}}
and
\pi_{t}=\frac{5}{t-t_{1}+50}
are nearly indistinguishable from those obtained with a constant exploration rate. This demonstrates that the dependence structure induced by the decision-making process provides sufficient implicit exploration to sustain accurate estimation, in line with the guarantees of Theorem
4
. Figure
5(b)
illustrates the cumulative regret under various exploration strategies. When the exploration rate is held constant, the regret grows linearly and is noticeably larger than under the decreasing exploration schedules
\pi_{t}=\frac{5}{\sqrt{t-t_{1}+50}}
or
\pi_{t}=\frac{5}{t-t_{1}+50}
. The superior performance of the decaying exploration rules is consistent with the theoretical guarantees established in Theorem
5
.

Appendix A
Proofs in Section
2

A.1
Conditional Orlicz Norm

In this section, we formally introduce the definition of conditional Orlicz norm. We need the following definition of essential supremum and we refer interested readers to
3
,
23
and
40
for more detailed discussion.

Definition 4

(Definition of Essential Supremum/Infimum)
.

Let
\Phi
be any set of random variables on
(\Omega,{\mathcal{F}},{\mathbb{P}})
.

1.

There exists a random variable
\varphi^{*}
satisfying the following two preperties.

(a)

\varphi^{*}\geq\varphi
P-a.s. for all
\varphi\in\Phi
;

(b)

for every
\psi\geq\varphi
P-a.s. for all
\varphi\in\Phi
, it has
\psi\geq\varphi^{*}
;

then
\varphi^{*}
is the essential supremum of
\Phi
. The essential infimum of
\Phi
is the essential supremum of
-\Phi
.

2.

Suppose in addition that
\Phi
is directed upward, i.e., for every
\varphi,\widetilde{\varphi}\in\Phi
there exists
\psi\in\Phi
such that
\psi\geq\varphi\vee\widetilde{\varphi}
. Then there exists an increasing sequence
\varphi_{1}\leq\varphi_{2}\leq\cdots
in
\Phi
such that
\varphi^{*}=\lim_{n\to+\infty}\varphi_{n}
P-almost surely.

Theorem 6

(Conditional Orlicz Norm)
.

Suppose
X
is a real valued random variable of
(\Omega,{\mathcal{F}}_{X},{\mathbb{P}})
. Then for any
{\mathcal{F}}\subseteq{\mathcal{F}}_{X}
, let
\Phi:=\{\varphi\in{\mathcal{F}}:\;\varphi\geq 0,\;{\mathbb{E}}\{\exp(|X/\varphi|^{\alpha})|{\mathcal{F}}\}\leq 2\}
. Define the conditional Orlicz norm as follows,

\displaystyle\|X|{\mathcal{F}}\|_{\Psi_{\alpha}}:=\operatorname{ess\,inf}\Phi.

Then for
\alpha\geq 1
,
\|\cdot|{\mathcal{F}}\|_{\Psi_{\alpha}}
is well defined as a norm and satisfies
{\mathbb{E}}\{\exp(|X/\|X|{\mathcal{F}}\|_{\Psi_{\alpha}}|^{\alpha})|{\mathcal{F}}\}\leq 2
.

The proof is adapted from the proof for Orlicz norm
\|\cdot\|_{\Psi_{\alpha}}
.

Proof.

We first prove
\|\cdot|{\mathcal{F}}\|_{\Psi_{\alpha}}
is a norm. It is obvious that for any scalar
a
, it has
\|aX|{\mathcal{F}}\|_{\Psi_{\alpha}}=|a|\cdot\|X|{\mathcal{F}}\|_{\Psi_{\alpha}}
. On the other hand, if
X=0
a.s., then
\|X|{\mathcal{F}}\|_{\Psi_{\alpha}}=0
a.s.. Conversely, if
\|X|{\mathcal{F}}\|_{\Psi_{\alpha}}=0
a.s., then let
A:=\{w:X(w)\neq 0\}
. By Jensen’s inequality, we have
\exp(({\mathbb{E}}\{|X|/K_{{\mathcal{F}}}|{\mathcal{F}}\})^{\alpha})\leq{\mathbb{E}}\left\{\exp((|X|/K_{{\mathcal{F}}})^{\alpha})\big|{\mathcal{F}}\right\}\leq 2
, and it implies
{\mathbb{E}}|X|=0
, which shows
X=0
almost surely. We only need to verify the triangle inequality. For any
\varepsilon>0
, let
u=\|X|{\mathcal{F}}\|_{\alpha}+\varepsilon
and
v=\|Y|{\mathcal{F}}\|_{\Psi_{\alpha}}+\varepsilon
, and it has

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\left\{\exp\left(\left(\frac{|X+Y|}{u+v}\right)^{\alpha}\right)\bigg|{\mathcal{F}}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq{\mathbb{E}}\left\{\exp\left(\left(\frac{|X|+|Y|}{u+v}\right)^{\alpha}\right)\bigg|{\mathcal{F}}\right\}

\displaystyle={\mathbb{E}}\left\{\exp\left(\left(\frac{u}{u+v}\frac{|X|}{u}+\frac{v}{u+v}\frac{|X|}{v}\right)^{\alpha}\right)\bigg|{\mathcal{F}}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{u}{u+v}{\mathbb{E}}\left\{\exp\left(\left(\frac{|X|}{u}\right)^{\alpha}\right)\bigg|{\mathcal{F}}\right\}+\frac{v}{u+v}{\mathbb{E}}\left\{\exp\left(\left(\frac{|X|}{v}\right)^{\alpha}\right)\bigg|{\mathcal{F}}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq 2,

which proves
\|\cdot|{\mathcal{F}}\|_{\Psi_{\alpha}}
is a norm. Moreover,
-\Phi
is directed upward and then by Definition
4
, we have the existence of a decreasing positive random variables
\varphi_{1}\geq\varphi_{2}\geq\cdots
in
\Phi
such that
\lim_{n\to+\infty}\varphi_{n}=\varphi^{*}:=\|X|{\mathcal{F}}\|_{\Psi_{\alpha}}
. Hence, by Fatou’s Lemma, we have

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\left\{\exp\left|\frac{X}{\|X|{\mathcal{F}}\|_{\Psi_{\alpha}}}\right|^{\alpha}|{\mathcal{F}}\right\}=\lim_{n\to+\infty}{\mathbb{E}}\left\{\exp\left|\frac{X}{\varphi_{n}}\right|^{\alpha}|{\mathcal{F}}\right\}\leq 2.

∎

Lemma 7

(Preliminary)
.

Suppose
{\mathbf{X}},{\mathbf{Y}}
are random vectors and
{\mathcal{F}}
is a sigma field, then we have

\displaystyle\|{\mathbf{X}}{\mathbf{Y}}^{\top}|{\mathcal{F}}\|_{\Psi_{1}}\leq\|{\mathbf{X}}|{\mathcal{F}}\|_{\Psi_{2}}\cdot\|{\mathbf{Y}}|{\mathcal{F}}\|_{\Psi_{2}}.

If
X\perp Y|{\mathcal{F}}
, then
{\mathbb{E}}[XY|{\mathcal{F}}]={\mathbb{E}}[X|{\mathcal{F}}]\cdot{\mathbb{E}}[Y|{\mathcal{F}}]
and
{\mathbb{E}}[X|Y,{\mathcal{F}}]={\mathbb{E}}[X|{\mathcal{F}}]
,
a.s.
. If
\|X|{\mathcal{F}}\|_{\Psi_{\alpha}}\leq K
holds
a.s.
, and
K
is a constant, then
\|X\|_{\Psi_{\alpha}}\leq K
. If
X
is independent of
{\mathcal{F}}
, then
\|X|{\mathcal{F}}\|_{\Psi_{1}}=\|X\|_{\Psi_{1}}
,
a.s.
. If
X,Y
are nonnegative nonnegative random variables and
|Y|\leq K
a.s., where
K
is a constant, then
\|XY|{\mathcal{F}}\|_{\Psi_{\alpha}}\leq K\|X|{\mathcal{F}}\|
.

Lemma 8
.

If
{\mathcal{F}}_{1}\subseteq{\mathcal{F}}_{2}
are sigma fields and
\|{\mathbf{X}}|{\mathcal{F}}_{2}\|_{\Psi_{2}}\leq K
for some constant
K
, then
\|{\mathbf{X}}|{\mathcal{F}}_{1}\|_{\Psi_{2}}\leq K
. Additionally, for
{\mathbf{X}}\in{\mathcal{F}}_{2}
with
\|{\mathbf{X}}|{\mathcal{F}}_{1}\|_{\Psi_{2}}\leq\lambda
and for
\|\xi|{\mathcal{F}}_{2}\|_{\Psi_{2}}\leq K
, then we have
\|\xi{\mathbf{X}}|{\mathcal{F}}_{1}\|_{\Psi_{1}}\leq K\lambda
.

Afterwards, the properties of conditional Orlicz norm are used as knowledge.

A.2
Proof of Theorem
1

In this section, we only prove the constant stepsize scheme
\eta_{t}:=\eta
of Theorem
1
. We defer the remaining proof to Section
C
, where a more general convergence is proved and the second part of Theorem
1
is included as a special case.

Proof of Theorem
1
(i).

We prove by induction. Denote the event

{\mathcal{E}}_{t}:=\left\{\|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\|^{2}\leq C^{*}(1-c)^{2t}\|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{0}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\|^{2}+2\frac{\lambda_{\max}}{\lambda_{\min}}\eta\sigma^{2}d\right\},

where values of
c,C^{*}
will be specified later. Obviously, the event holds with
t=0
. We proceed to prove the convergence dynamics of
{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t+1}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}
under
\cup_{l=0}^{t}{\mathcal{E}}_{l}
. Let
{\mathcal{E}}_{t}^{X}:=\left\{\|{\mathbf{X}}_{t}\|^{2}\leq 2\lambda_{\max}d\right\}
. Assumption
1
implies
{\mathbb{P}}({\mathcal{E}}_{t}^{X}|{\mathcal{F}}_{t})\geq 1-\exp(-Cd)
. According to the update Equation (
3
), we can write the estimation error as follows,

\displaystyle\left\|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t+1}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\right\|^{2}=\left\|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\right\|^{2}-2\eta\left({\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\right)^{\top}{\mathbf{X}}_{t}\left({\mathbf{X}}_{t}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t}-Y_{t}\right)+\eta^{2}\left({\mathbf{X}}_{t}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t}-Y_{t}\right)^{2}\left\|{\mathbf{X}}_{t}\right\|^{2}.

We first consider its expectation conditional on
{\mathcal{F}}_{t}^{+}
. Assumption
2
implies that
{\mathbb{E}}\left\{\xi_{t}|{\mathcal{F}}_{t}^{+}\right\}=0
. Hence, we have

{\mathbb{E}}\left\{\left\|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t+1}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\right\|^{2}\big|{\mathcal{F}}_{t}^{+}\right\}\\
\leq\left\|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\right\|^{2}-2\eta\left({\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\right)^{\top}{\mathbf{X}}_{t}{\mathbf{X}}_{t}^{\top}\left({\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\right)+\eta^{2}\left({\mathbf{X}}_{t}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t}-{\mathbf{X}}_{t}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\right)^{2}\|{\mathbf{X}}_{t}\|^{2}+\eta^{2}\sigma^{2}\|{\mathbf{X}}_{t}\|^{2}.

We further consider its expectation conditional on
{\mathcal{F}}_{t}
. By Assumption
1
, we have

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\left\{\left\|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t+1}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\right\|^{2}\big|{\mathcal{F}}_{t}\right\}\leq\left(1-2\eta\lambda_{\min}+\eta^{2}\lambda_{\max}^{2}d\right)\left\|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\right\|^{2}+\eta^{2}\sigma^{2}\lambda_{\max}d.

We then consider the difference between
\left\|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t+1}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\right\|^{2}
and
{\mathbb{E}}\left\{\left\|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t+1}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\right\|^{2}\big|{\mathcal{F}}_{t}\right\}
,

\displaystyle\left\|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t+1}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle-{\mathbb{E}}\left\{\left\|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t+1}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\right\|^{2}\big|{\mathcal{F}}_{t}\right\}=\underbrace{2\eta\cdot\xi_{t}\left({\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\right)^{\top}{\mathbf{X}}_{t}}_{I_{1}^{(t)}}

\displaystyle\underbrace{-2\eta\left({\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\right)^{\top}\left\{{\mathbf{X}}_{t}{\mathbf{X}}_{t}^{\top}-{\mathbb{E}}\left\{{\mathbf{X}}_{t}{\mathbf{X}}_{t}^{\top}\big|{\mathcal{F}}_{t}\right\}\right\}\left({\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\right)}_{I_{2}^{(t)}}+\underbrace{\eta^{2}\left\{\xi_{t}^{2}\|{\mathbf{X}}_{t}\|^{2}-{\mathbb{E}}\left\{\xi_{t}^{2}\|{\mathbf{X}}_{t}\|^{2}\big|{\mathcal{F}}_{t}\right\}\right\}}_{I_{3}^{(t)}}

\displaystyle+\underbrace{\eta^{2}\left\{\left({\mathbf{X}}_{t}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t}-{\mathbf{X}}_{t}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\right)^{2}\|{\mathbf{X}}_{t}\|^{2}-{\mathbb{E}}\left\{\left({\mathbf{X}}_{t}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t}-{\mathbf{X}}_{t}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\right)^{2}\|{\mathbf{X}}_{t}\|^{2}\big|{\mathcal{F}}_{t}\right\}\right\}}_{I_{4}^{(t)}}.

Then, we decompose the estimation error as follows,

\displaystyle\|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t+1}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\|^{2}

\displaystyle={\mathbb{E}}\left\{\left\|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t+1}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\right\|^{2}\big|{\mathcal{F}}_{t}\right\}+\left\{\left\|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t+1}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\right\|^{2}-{\mathbb{E}}\left\{\left\|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t+1}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\right\|^{2}\big|{\mathcal{F}}_{t}\right\}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq\left(1-2\eta\lambda_{\min}+\eta^{2}\lambda_{\max}^{2}d\right)\left\|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\right\|^{2}+\eta^{2}\sigma^{2}\lambda_{\max}d+I_{1}^{(t)}+I_{2}^{(t)}+I_{3}^{(t)}+I_{4}^{(t)}.

For
\eta\leq\lambda_{\min}/(d\lambda_{\max}^{2})
, we have

\displaystyle\|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t+1}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\|^{2}\leq\left(1-\eta\lambda_{\min}\right)\left\|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\right\|^{2}+\eta^{2}\sigma^{2}\lambda_{\max}d+I_{1}^{(t)}+I_{2}^{(t)}+I_{3}^{(t)}+I_{4}^{(t)}.

We then accumulate the above equation until
t=0
,

\begin{split}&\|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t+1}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\|^{2}\leq\left(1-\eta\lambda_{\min}\right)^{t+1}\left\|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{0}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\right\|^{2}+\sum_{l=0}^{t}\left(1-\eta\lambda_{\min}\right)^{t-l}I_{1}^{(l)}+\sum_{l=0}^{t}\left(1-\eta\lambda_{\min}\right)^{t-l}I_{2}^{(l)}\\
&~~~~~~~~~~~~~~+\sum_{l=0}^{t}\left(1-\eta\lambda_{\min}\right)^{t-l}I_{3}^{(l)}+\sum_{l=0}^{t}\left(1-\eta\lambda_{\min}\right)^{t-l}I_{4}^{(l)}+\eta^{2}\sigma^{2}\lambda_{\max}d\sum_{l=0}^{t}\left(1-\eta\lambda_{\min}\right)^{l}.\end{split}

(18)

We then bound each term of the RHS for Equation
18
. It’s worth noting that

\left(1-\eta\lambda_{\min}\right)^{t+1}\leq\left(1-\eta\lambda_{\min}/2\right)^{2(t+1)},\quad\eta^{2}\sigma^{2}\lambda_{\max}d\sum_{l=0}^{t}\left(1-\eta\lambda_{\min}\right)^{l}\leq\frac{\lambda_{\max}}{\lambda_{\min}}\eta\sigma^{2}d.

We then only need to bound the cumulated term for
I_{1},I_{2},I_{3},I_{4}
. First consider the
I_{1}
term, as
{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t}\in{\mathcal{F}}_{t}
, and we have

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\left\{\xi_{l}\left({\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{l}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\right)^{\top}{\mathbf{X}}_{l}\big|{\mathcal{F}}_{l}\right\}=0,\quad\left\|\xi_{l}\left({\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{l}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\right)^{\top}{\mathbf{X}}_{l}\big|{\mathcal{F}}_{l}\right\|_{\Psi_{1}}\leq\sqrt{\lambda_{\max}}\sigma\|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{l}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\|.

Additionally, under the event
{\mathcal{E}}_{l}
,
\|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{l}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\|^{2}\leq C^{*}(1-c)^{2l}\|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{l}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\|^{2}+2\frac{\lambda_{\max}}{\lambda_{\min}}\eta\sigma^{2}d
. We then use technical Lemma
9
. Thus, under the event
\cup_{l=0}^{t}{\mathcal{E}}_{l}
, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{l=0}^{t}\left\|(1-\eta\lambda_{\min})^{t-l}I_{1}^{(l)}\big|{\mathcal{F}}_{l}\right\|_{\Psi_{1}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\eta^{2}\sigma^{2}\lambda_{\max}\sum_{l=0}^{t}(1-\eta\lambda_{\min})^{2t-2l}((1-c)^{2l}\|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{0}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\|^{2}+\frac{\lambda_{\max}}{\lambda_{\min}}d\eta\sigma^{2})

\displaystyle\leq C\eta^{2}(1-c)^{2t}\frac{1}{1-\frac{1-\eta\lambda_{\min}}{1-c}}\|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{0}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\|^{2}+d\eta^{3}\sigma^{4}\frac{\lambda_{\max}^{2}}{\lambda_{\min}(1-(1-\eta\lambda_{\min})^{2})}

\displaystyle\leq C\frac{\eta\lambda_{\max}\sigma^{2}}{\lambda_{\min}}(1-c)^{2t}\|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{0}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\|^{2}+d\frac{\lambda_{\max}^{2}\eta^{2}\sigma^{4}}{\lambda_{\min}^{2}},

where the last line is due to
4c\leq\eta\lambda_{\min}
. Additionally, we have

\displaystyle\max_{l}\left\|(1-\eta\lambda_{\min})^{t-l}I_{1}^{(l)}\big|{\mathcal{F}}_{l}\right\|_{\Psi_{1}}\leq C\eta\sigma\sqrt{\lambda_{\max}}\left((1-c)^{t}\|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{0}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\|+\sqrt{\frac{\lambda_{\max}}{\lambda_{\min}}}\sqrt{\eta}\sqrt{d}\sigma\right).

Thus, by Lemma
9
, we have

{\mathbb{P}}\left(\left|\sum_{l=0}^{t}\left(1-\eta\lambda_{\min}\right)^{t-l}I_{1}^{(l)}\right|\geq s\right)\leq\exp\left(-\min\left\{\frac{s^{2}}{C\left(\frac{\sigma^{2}\eta\lambda_{\max}}{\lambda_{\min}}(1-c)^{2t}\|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{0}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\|^{2}+d\frac{\eta^{2}\sigma^{4}\lambda_{\max}^{2}}{\lambda_{\min}^{2}}\right)},\right.\right.\\
\left.\left.\frac{s}{\eta\sigma\sqrt{\lambda_{\max}}\left((1-c)^{t}\|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{0}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\|+\sqrt{\frac{\lambda_{\max}}{\lambda_{\min}}}\sqrt{\eta}\sqrt{d}\sigma\right)}\right\}\right).

We insert
s=(1-c)^{2t+2}\|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{0}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\|^{2}+\frac{\lambda_{\max}}{\lambda_{\min}}\eta\sigma^{2}d\geq 2(1-c)^{t+1}\sqrt{\frac{\lambda_{\max}}{\lambda_{\min}}}\sqrt{\eta d}\|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{0}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\|\sigma
into the above equation and it yields

{\mathbb{P}}\left(\left|\sum_{l=0}^{t}\left(1-\eta\lambda_{\min}\right)^{t-l}I_{1}^{(l)}\right|\geq(1-c)^{2t+2}\|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{0}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\|^{2}+\frac{\lambda_{\max}}{\lambda_{\min}}\eta\sigma^{2}d\right)\\
\leq\exp\left(-c_{1}\min\left\{d,\sqrt{\frac{1}{\lambda_{\min}}\frac{d}{\eta}}\right\}\right)

We then consider the term
I_{2}
. Assumption
1
guarantees,

\displaystyle\left\|\left({\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{l}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\right)^{\top}\left\{{\mathbf{X}}_{l}{\mathbf{X}}_{l}^{\top}-{\mathbb{E}}\left\{{\mathbf{X}}_{l}{\mathbf{X}}_{l}^{\top}\big|{\mathcal{F}}_{l}\right\}\right\}\left({\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{l}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\right)\big|{\mathcal{F}}_{l}\right\|_{\Psi_{1}}\leq\lambda_{\max}\|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{l}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\|^{2}.

Under the event
\cup_{l=0}^{t}{\mathcal{E}}_{l}
, we have that

\displaystyle\sum_{l=0}^{t}\left\|(1-\eta\lambda_{\min})^{t-l}I_{2}^{(l)}\big|{\mathcal{F}}_{l}\right\|_{\Psi_{1}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\eta^{2}\lambda_{\max}^{2}\sum_{l=0}^{t}(1-\eta\lambda_{\min})^{2t-2l}\left((1-c)^{4l}\|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{0}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\|^{4}+\frac{\lambda_{\max}^{2}}{\lambda_{\min}^{2}}\eta^{2}\sigma^{4}d^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq C\eta\frac{\lambda_{\max}^{2}}{\lambda_{\min}}(1-c)^{4t}\|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{0}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\|^{4}+C\frac{\lambda_{\max}^{4}}{\lambda_{\min}^{3}}\eta^{3}\sigma^{4}d^{2},

and

\displaystyle\max_{l}\left\|(1-\eta\lambda_{\min})^{t-l}I_{2}^{(l)}\big|{\mathcal{F}}_{l}\right\|_{\Psi_{1}}\leq C\eta\lambda_{\max}(1-c)^{2t}\|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{0}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\|^{2}+\eta^{2}\frac{\lambda_{\max}^{2}}{\lambda_{\min}}\sigma^{2}d.

Then follow Lemma
9
and select
s=(1-c)^{2t+2}\|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{0}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\|^{2}+\frac{\lambda_{\max}}{\lambda_{\min}}\eta\sigma^{2}d
, we have

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}\left(\left|\sum_{l=0}^{t}\left(1-\eta\lambda_{\min}\right)^{t-l}I_{1}^{(l)}\right|\geq(1-c)^{2t+2}\|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{0}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\|^{2}+\frac{\lambda_{\max}}{\lambda_{\min}}\eta\sigma^{2}d\right)\leq\exp\left(-c_{1}\frac{\lambda_{\min}}{\lambda_{\max}^{2}}\frac{1}{\eta}\right).

We then consider
I_{3}
. Under
{\mathcal{E}}_{l}^{X}
, by Assumption
2
, we have

\displaystyle\left\|\left\{\xi_{l}^{2}\|{\mathbf{X}}_{l}\|^{2}-{\mathbb{E}}\left\{\xi_{l}^{2}\|{\mathbf{X}}_{l}\|^{2}\big|{\mathcal{F}}_{l}\right\}\right\}\big|{\mathcal{F}}_{l}\right\|_{\Psi_{1}}\leq C\lambda_{\max}\sigma^{2}d.

Under the event
\cup_{l=0}^{t}{\mathcal{E}}_{l}^{X}
, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{l=0}^{t}\left\|(1-\eta\lambda_{\min})^{t-l}I_{3}^{(l)}\big|{\mathcal{F}}_{l}\right\|_{\Psi_{1}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{l=0}^{t}(1-\eta\lambda_{\min})^{2t-2l}\eta^{4}\lambda_{\max}^{2}\sigma^{4}d^{2}\leq\frac{\lambda_{\max}^{2}}{\lambda_{\min}}\eta^{3}d^{2}\sigma^{4},

and

\displaystyle\max_{l}\left\|(1-\eta\lambda_{\min})^{t-l}I_{3}^{(l)}\big|{\mathcal{F}}_{l}\right\|_{\Psi_{1}}\leq\eta^{2}\lambda_{\max}\sigma^{2}d.

Follow Lemma
9
and then we insert
s=(1-c)^{2t+2}\|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{0}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\|^{2}+\frac{\lambda_{\max}}{\lambda_{\min}}\eta\sigma^{2}d
,

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}\left(\left|\sum_{l=0}^{t}\left(1-\eta\lambda_{\min}\right)^{t-l}I_{3}^{(l)}\right|\geq(1-c)^{2t+2}\|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{0}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\|^{2}+\frac{\lambda_{\max}}{\lambda_{\min}}\eta\sigma^{2}d\right)\leq\exp\left(-c_{1}\frac{1}{\lambda_{\max}}\frac{1}{\eta}\right).

Finally, we consider
I_{4}
. Under the event
{\mathcal{E}}_{t}^{X}
, we have

\displaystyle\left\|\left\{\left({\mathbf{X}}_{l}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{l}-{\mathbf{X}}_{t}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\right)^{2}\|{\mathbf{X}}_{l}\|^{2}-{\mathbb{E}}\left\{\left({\mathbf{X}}_{l}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{l}-{\mathbf{X}}_{l}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\right)^{2}\|{\mathbf{X}}_{l}\|^{2}\big|{\mathcal{F}}_{l}\right\}\right\}\big|{\mathcal{F}}_{l}\right\|_{\Psi_{1}}\leq C\lambda_{\max}^{2}d\|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{l}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\|^{2}.

Under the event
\cup_{l=0}^{t}{\mathcal{E}}_{l}^{X}
, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{l=0}^{t}\left\|(1-\eta\lambda_{\min})^{t-l}I_{4}^{(l)}\big|{\mathcal{F}}_{l}\right\|_{\Psi_{1}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\eta^{4}\lambda_{\max}^{4}d^{2}\sum_{l=0}^{t}(1-\eta\lambda_{\min})^{2t-2l}\left((1-c)^{4l}\|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{0}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\|^{4}+\frac{\lambda_{\max}^{2}}{\lambda_{\min}^{2}}\eta^{2}\sigma^{4}d^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq C\frac{\lambda_{\max}^{4}}{\lambda_{\min}}\eta^{3}d^{2}\|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{0}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\|^{4}+C\frac{\lambda_{\max}^{6}}{\lambda_{\min}^{3}}\eta^{5}\sigma^{4}d^{4},

and

\displaystyle\max_{l}\left\|(1-\eta\lambda_{\min})^{t-l}I_{4}^{(l)}\big|{\mathcal{F}}_{l}\right\|_{\Psi_{1}}\leq C\eta^{2}\lambda_{\max}^{2}d(1-c)^{2t}\|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{0}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\|^{2}+C\eta^{3}(1-c)^{2t}\frac{\lambda_{\max}^{3}}{\lambda_{\min}}\sigma^{2}d^{2}.

Insert
s=(1-c)^{2t+2}\|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{0}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\|^{2}+\frac{\lambda_{\max}}{\lambda_{\min}}\eta\sigma^{2}d
into Lemma
9
, which leads to

{\mathbb{P}}\left(\left|\sum_{l=0}^{t}\left(1-\eta\lambda_{\min}\right)^{t-l}I_{4}^{(l)}\right|\geq(1-c)^{2t+2}\|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{0}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\|^{2}+\frac{\lambda_{\max}}{\lambda_{\min}}\eta\sigma^{2}d\right)\\
\leq\exp\left(-\min\left\{\frac{\lambda_{\min}}{\lambda_{\max}^{4}\eta^{3}d^{2}},\frac{1}{\lambda_{\max}^{2}\eta^{2}d}\right\}\right).

Thus, Equation (
18
) arrives at,

\displaystyle\|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t+1}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\|^{2}\leq 5(1-c)^{2t+2}\|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{0}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\|^{2}+5\frac{\lambda_{\max}}{\lambda_{\min}}\eta\sigma^{2}d,

with probability over
1-5\exp(-c_{1}d)
. And
c
is any constant satisfying
c\leq\eta\lambda_{\min}/2
.
∎

Appendix B
Proofs in Section
3

This section presents the proofs of sparse regression, specifically, consisting of Lemma
1
, Lemma
2
and Proposition
1
.

B.1
Proof of Lemma
1

First of all, the SGD update with a fixed support
{\mathcal{S}}_{0}
can be written as,

\displaystyle{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t+1}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}={\mathcal{H}}_{{\mathcal{S}}_{0}}\left({\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t}-\eta_{t}\cdot\left({\mathbf{X}}_{t}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t}-Y_{t}\right){\mathbf{X}}_{t}\right)-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}={\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}-\eta_{t}\cdot\left({\mathbf{X}}_{t}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t}-Y_{t}\right){\mathcal{H}}_{{\mathcal{S}}_{0}}({\mathbf{X}}_{t}).

It’s worth noting that
{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}
is supported on
{\mathcal{S}}^{*}\cup{\mathcal{S}}_{0}
, where
{\mathcal{S}}^{*}\cup{\mathcal{S}}_{0}=({\mathcal{S}}^{*}\setminus{\mathcal{S}}_{0})\cup{\mathcal{S}}_{0}
. For one thing, the iterates restricted on
{\mathcal{S}}^{*}\setminus{\mathcal{S}}_{0}
satisfy

\displaystyle\left[{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t+1}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\right]_{{\mathcal{S}}^{*}\setminus{\mathcal{S}}_{0}}=\left[{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\right]_{{\mathcal{S}}^{*}\setminus{\mathcal{S}}_{0}}=[-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}]_{{\mathcal{S}}^{*}\setminus{\mathcal{S}}_{0}},

while for entries on
{\mathcal{S}}_{0}
, it is

\displaystyle\left[{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t+1}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\right]_{{\mathcal{S}}_{0}}=[{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}]_{{\mathcal{S}}_{0}}-\eta_{t}[{\mathbf{X}}_{t}]_{{\mathcal{S}}_{0}}({\mathbf{X}}_{t}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t}-Y_{t}).

We are going to prove Lemma
1
by induction. Define the event

{\mathcal{E}}_{t}:=\left\{\|[{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}]_{{\mathcal{S}}_{0}}\|^{2}\leq C^{*}\frac{s\log(2d/s)+\delta_{t}}{t+C_{b}s\log(2d/s)}\frac{\sigma^{2}}{\lambda_{\min}}+C\left(\frac{\lambda_{\max,s}^{\textsf{Off}}}{\lambda_{\min}}\right)^{2}\cdot\|[{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}]_{{\mathcal{S}}^{*}\setminus{\mathcal{S}}_{0}}\|^{2}\right\},

where
C^{*}
is some constant that does not depend on
t,d,s
, having its close form in Lemma
1
. It is obvious that
{\mathcal{E}}_{0}
holds. We will prove
{\mathcal{E}}_{t+1}
holds with high probability under
\cup_{l=0}^{t}{\mathcal{E}}_{l}
. First of all, the squared estimation error supported on
{\mathcal{S}}_{0}
has

\|[{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t+1}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}]_{{\mathcal{S}}_{0}}\|^{2}=\|[{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}]_{{\mathcal{S}}_{0}}\|^{2}-2\eta_{t}({\mathbf{X}}_{t}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t}-Y_{t})[{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}]_{{\mathcal{S}}_{0}}^{\top}[{\mathbf{X}}_{t}]_{{\mathcal{S}}_{0}}\\
+\eta_{t}^{2}({\mathbf{X}}_{t}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t}-Y_{t})^{2}\|[{\mathbf{X}}_{t}]_{{\mathcal{S}}_{0}}\|^{2}.

We remark that
{\mathbf{X}}_{t}
might depend on
{\mathcal{S}}_{0}
. Based on Assumption
3
and Lemma
13
, we have
{\mathbb{E}}\{\|[{\mathbf{X}}_{t}]_{{\mathcal{S}}_{0}}\|^{4}|{\mathcal{F}}_{t}\}\leq s^{2}\log^{2}(2d/s)\lambda_{\max}^{2}
and, moreover, we have

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\left\{({\mathbf{X}}_{t}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t}-Y_{t})^{2}\|[{\mathbf{X}}_{t}]_{{\mathcal{S}}_{0}}\|^{2}\big|{\mathcal{F}}_{t}\right\}={\mathbb{E}}\left\{({\mathbf{X}}_{t}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t}-{\mathbf{X}}_{t}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*})^{2}\|[{\mathbf{X}}_{t}]_{{\mathcal{S}}_{0}}\|^{2}\big|{\mathcal{F}}_{t}\right\}+{\mathbb{E}}\left\{\xi_{t}^{2}\|[{\mathbf{X}}_{t}]_{{\mathcal{S}}_{0}}\|^{2}\big|{\mathcal{F}}_{t}\right\}

\displaystyle~~~~~\leq\frac{s\log(2d/s)}{2\|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\|^{2}}{\mathbb{E}}\left\{({\mathbf{X}}_{t}{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t}-{\mathbf{X}}_{t}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*})^{4}\big|{\mathcal{F}}_{t}\right\}+\frac{\|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\|^{2}}{2s\log(2d/s)}{\mathbb{E}}\left\{\|[{\mathbf{X}}_{t}]_{{\mathcal{S}}_{0}}\|^{4}\big|{\mathcal{F}}_{t}\right\}+\sigma^{2}s\log(2d/s)\lambda_{\max}

\displaystyle~~~~~\leq\lambda_{\max}^{2}s\log(2d/s)\|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\|^{2}+2\sigma^{2}s\log(2d/s)\lambda_{\max}\leq 3\sigma^{2}s\log(2d/s)\lambda_{\max},

where the second line uses the inequality
a^{2}+b^{2}\geq 2ab
. Thus, the conditional expectation of
\|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t+1}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\|^{2}
satisfies the following equation,

{\mathbb{E}}\left\{\left\|[{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t+1}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}]_{{\mathcal{S}}_{0}}\right\|^{2}\big|{\mathcal{F}}_{t}\right\}\leq\left(1-2\eta_{t}\lambda_{\min}\right)\left\|\left[{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\right]_{{\mathcal{S}}_{0}}\right\|^{2}+3\eta_{t}^{2}\lambda_{\max}\cdot s\log(2d/s)\sigma^{2}\\
+2\eta_{t}\lambda_{\max,s}^{\textsf{Off}}\cdot\|\left[{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\right]_{{\mathcal{S}}_{0}}\|\cdot\|[{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}]_{{\mathcal{S}}^{*}\setminus{\mathcal{S}}_{0}}\|.

We bound the third term of the RHS with
2\lambda_{\max,s}^{\textsf{Off}}\cdot\|\left[{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\right]_{{\mathcal{S}}_{0}}\|\cdot\|[{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}]_{{\mathcal{S}}^{*}\setminus{\mathcal{S}}_{0}}\|\leq\lambda_{\min}\|[{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}]_{{\mathcal{S}}_{0}}\|^{2}+(\lambda_{\max,s}^{\textsf{Off}})^{2}\|[{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}]_{{\mathcal{S}}^{*}\setminus{\mathcal{S}}_{0}}\|^{2}/\lambda_{\min}
. After simplifications, it arrives at

{\mathbb{E}}\left\{\left\|[{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t+1}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}]_{{\mathcal{S}}_{0}}\right\|^{2}\big|{\mathcal{F}}_{t}\right\}\leq\left(1-\eta_{t}\lambda_{\min}\right)\left\|\left[{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\right]_{{\mathcal{S}}_{0}}\right\|^{2}\\
+3\eta_{t}^{2}\lambda_{\max}s\log(2d/s)\sigma^{2}+\eta_{t}\|[{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}]_{{\mathcal{S}}^{*}\setminus{\mathcal{S}}_{0}}\|^{2}\cdot\frac{(\lambda_{\max,s}^{\textsf{Off}})^{2}}{\lambda_{\min}}.

We then decompose the difference between the squared estimation error and the conditional expectation,

\displaystyle\|[{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t+1}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}]_{{\mathcal{S}}_{0}}\|^{2}-{\mathbb{E}}\left\{\left\|[{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t+1}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}]_{{\mathcal{S}}_{0}}\right\|^{2}\big|{\mathcal{F}}_{t}\right\}

\displaystyle~~~~~~=-2\eta_{t}\underbrace{\left\{[{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}]_{{\mathcal{S}}_{0}}^{\top}[{\mathbf{X}}_{t}]_{{\mathcal{S}}_{0}}{\mathbf{X}}_{t}^{\top}({\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*})-{\mathbb{E}}\left\{[{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}]_{{\mathcal{S}}_{0}}^{\top}[{\mathbf{X}}_{t}]_{{\mathcal{S}}_{0}}{\mathbf{X}}_{t}^{\top}({\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*})\big|{\mathcal{F}}_{t}\right\}\right\}}_{\boldsymbol{\varXi}_{1}^{(t)}}

\displaystyle~~~~~~~+2\eta_{t}\underbrace{\left\{\xi_{t}[{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}]_{{\mathcal{S}}_{0}}^{\top}[{\mathbf{X}}_{t}]_{{\mathcal{S}}_{0}}-{\mathbb{E}}\left\{\xi_{t}[{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}]_{{\mathcal{S}}_{0}}^{\top}[{\mathbf{X}}_{t}]_{{\mathcal{S}}_{0}}\big|{\mathcal{F}}_{t}\right\}\right\}}_{\boldsymbol{\varXi}_{2}^{(t)}}

\displaystyle~~~~~~~+\eta_{t}^{2}\underbrace{\left\{({\mathbf{X}}_{t}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t}-Y_{t})^{2}\|[{\mathbf{X}}_{t}]_{{\mathcal{S}}_{0}}\|^{2}-{\mathbb{E}}\left\{({\mathbf{X}}_{t}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t}-Y_{t})^{2}\|[{\mathbf{X}}_{t}]_{{\mathcal{S}}_{0}}\|^{2}\big|{\mathcal{F}}_{t}\right\}\right\}}_{\boldsymbol{\varXi}_{3}^{(t)}}.

It is obvious that the three terms
\boldsymbol{\varXi}_{i}^{(t)}
are all martingale differences, with conditional Orlicz norm bounded with

\displaystyle\|\boldsymbol{\varXi}_{1}^{(t)}|{\mathcal{F}}_{t}\|_{\Psi_{1}}\leq\lambda_{\max}\|[{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}]_{{\mathcal{S}}_{0}}\|^{2}+\lambda_{\max,s}^{\textsf{Off}}\cdot\|[{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}]_{{\mathcal{S}}_{0}}\|\cdot\|[{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}]_{{\mathcal{S}}^{*}\setminus{\mathcal{S}}_{0}}\|,

and

\displaystyle\|\boldsymbol{\varXi}_{2}^{(t)}|{\mathcal{F}}_{t}\|_{\Psi_{1}}\leq\sqrt{\lambda_{\max}}\sigma\|[{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}]_{{\mathcal{S}}_{0}}\|.

Denote
{\mathcal{E}}_{t}^{X}:=\{\|[{\mathbf{X}}_{t}]_{{\mathcal{S}}_{0}}\|^{2}\leq C\lambda_{\max}(s\log(2d/s)+\delta_{t})\}
and by Lemma
13
, we have
{\mathbb{P}}({\mathcal{E}}_{t}^{X})\geq 1-\exp(-c_{2}(s\log(2d/s)+\delta_{t}))
. Thus, under
{\mathcal{E}}_{t}^{X}
, we have

\displaystyle\|\boldsymbol{\varXi}_{3}^{(t)}|{\mathcal{F}}_{t},{\mathcal{E}}_{t}^{X}\|_{\Psi_{1}}\leq C(s\log(2d/s)+\delta_{t})\lambda_{\max}\sigma^{2}.

Thus, putting the conditional expectation together with the difference, we have

\|[{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t+1}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}]_{{\mathcal{S}}_{0}}\|^{2}={\mathbb{E}}\left\{\|[{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t+1}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}]_{{\mathcal{S}}_{0}}\|^{2}\big|{\mathcal{F}}_{t}\right\}+\left\{\|[{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t+1}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}]_{{\mathcal{S}}_{0}}\|^{2}-{\mathbb{E}}\left\{\|[{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t+1}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}]_{{\mathcal{S}}_{0}}\|^{2}\big|{\mathcal{F}}_{t}\right\}\right\}\\
\leq\left(1-\eta_{t}\lambda_{\min}\right)\left\|\left[{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\right]_{{\mathcal{S}}_{0}}\right\|^{2}+3\eta_{t}^{2}\lambda_{\max}s\log(2d/s)\sigma^{2}\\
+\eta_{t}\|[{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}]_{{\mathcal{S}}^{*}\setminus{\mathcal{S}}_{0}}\|^{2}\cdot\frac{(\lambda_{\max,s}^{\textsf{Off}})^{2}}{\lambda_{\min}}-2\eta_{t}\boldsymbol{\varXi}_{1}^{(t)}+2\eta_{t}\boldsymbol{\varXi}_{2}^{(t)}+\eta_{t}^{2}\boldsymbol{\varXi}_{3}^{(t)}.

Insert the stepsize
\eta_{t}=\frac{C_{a}}{\lambda_{\min}}\frac{1}{t+C_{b}s\log(2d/s)}
into the above equation and then we have

\|[{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t+1}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}]_{{\mathcal{S}}_{0}}\|^{2}\leq\underbrace{\prod_{j=0}^{t}\left(1-\frac{C_{a}}{j+C_{b}s\log(2d/s)}\right)\left\|\left[{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{0}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\right]_{{\mathcal{S}}_{0}}\right\|^{2}}_{\boldsymbol{\mathit{A}}_{1}}\\
+\underbrace{3s\log(2d/s)C_{a}^{2}\frac{\lambda_{\max}}{\lambda_{\min}^{2}}\sum_{l=0}^{t}\prod_{j=l}^{t}\left(1-\frac{C_{a}}{j+C_{b}s\log(2d/s)}\right)\left(\frac{1}{l+C_{b}s\log(2d/s)}\right)^{2}\sigma^{2}}_{\boldsymbol{\mathit{A}}_{2}}\\
+\underbrace{C_{a}\sum_{l=0}^{t}\prod_{j=l}^{t}\left(1-\frac{C_{a}}{j+C_{b}s\log(2d/s)}\right)\frac{1}{l+C_{b}s\log(2d/s)}\|[{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}]_{{\mathcal{S}}^{*}\setminus{\mathcal{S}}_{0}}\|^{2}\cdot\frac{(\lambda_{\max,s}^{\textsf{Off}})^{2}}{\lambda_{\min}^{2}}}_{\boldsymbol{\mathit{A}}_{3}}\\
-\underbrace{2\frac{C_{a}}{\lambda_{\min}}\sum_{l=0}^{t}\prod_{j=l}^{t}\left(1-\frac{C_{a}}{j+C_{b}s\log(2d/s)}\right)\frac{1}{l+C_{b}s\log(2d/s)}\boldsymbol{\varXi}_{1}^{(l)}}_{\boldsymbol{\mathit{A}}_{4}}\\
+\underbrace{2\frac{C_{a}}{\lambda_{\min}}\sum_{l=0}^{t}\prod_{j=l}^{t}\left(1-\frac{C_{a}}{j+C_{b}s\log(2d/s)}\right)\frac{1}{l+C_{b}s\log(2d/s)}\boldsymbol{\varXi}_{2}^{(l)}}_{\boldsymbol{\mathit{A}}_{5}}\\
+\underbrace{\frac{C_{a}^{2}}{\lambda_{\min}^{2}}\sum_{l=0}^{t}\prod_{j=l}^{t}\left(1-\frac{C_{a}}{j+C_{b}s\log(2d/s)}\right)\frac{1}{(l+C_{b}s\log(2d/s))^{2}}\boldsymbol{\varXi}_{3}^{(l)}}_{\boldsymbol{\mathit{A}}_{6}}.

We then bound each of the terms respectively. First, consider
{\mathbf{A}}_{1}
. By Lemma
16
, we have

\displaystyle\prod_{j=0}^{t}\left(1-\frac{C_{a}}{j+C_{b}s\log(2d/s)}\right)\leq\left(\frac{C_{b}s\log(2d/s)}{t+1+C_{b}s\log(2d/s)}\right)^{C_{a}},

which implies
\boldsymbol{\mathit{A}}_{1}\leq\frac{C_{b}s\log(2d/s)}{t+C_{b}s\log(2d/s)}\|[{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{0}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}]_{{\mathcal{S}}_{0}}\|^{2}
. Then consider term
\boldsymbol{\mathit{A}}_{2}
. By Lemma
16
, we have
\prod_{j=l}^{t}\left(1-\frac{C_{a}}{j+C_{b}s\log(2d/s)}\right)\left(\frac{1}{l+C_{b}s\log(2d/s)}\right)^{2}\leq\frac{(l+C_{b}s\log(2d/s))^{C_{a}-2}}{(t+1+C_{b}s\log(2d/s))^{C_{a}}}
and Lemma
17
further implies
\sum_{l=0}^{t}\prod_{j=l}^{t}\left(1-\frac{C_{a}}{j+C_{b}s\log(2d/s)}\right)\frac{1}{(l+C_{b}s\log(2d/s))^{2}}\leq\frac{2/C_{a}}{t+1+C_{b}s\log(2d/s)}
. Thus, we have

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\mathit{A}}_{2}\leq C\frac{\lambda_{\max}}{\lambda_{\min}^{2}}\cdot\frac{C_{a}s\log(2d/s)}{t+1+C_{b}s\log(2d/s)}\sigma^{2}.

As for term
\boldsymbol{\mathit{A}}_{3}
, similarly, we have

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\mathit{A}}_{3}\leq C\frac{(\lambda_{\max,s}^{\textsf{Off}})^{2}}{\lambda_{\min}^{2}}\cdot\|[{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}]_{{\mathcal{S}}^{*}\setminus{\mathcal{S}}_{0}}\|^{2}.

We then consider
\boldsymbol{\mathit{A}}_{4}
. We notice that

\left\|\prod_{j=l}^{t}\left(1-\frac{C_{a}}{j+C_{b}s\log(2d/s)}\right)\frac{1}{l+C_{b}s\log(2d/s)}\boldsymbol{\varXi}_{1}^{(l)}\bigg|{\mathcal{F}}_{l}\right\|_{\Psi_{1}}\\
\leq\frac{(l+C_{b}s\log(2d/s))^{C_{a}-1}}{(t+1+C_{b}s\log(2d/s))^{C_{a}}}\left(\lambda_{\max}\|[{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{l}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}]_{{\mathcal{S}}_{0}}\|^{2}+\frac{(\lambda_{\max,s}^{\textsf{Off}})^{2}}{\lambda_{\max}}\|[{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}]_{{\mathcal{S}}^{*}\setminus{\mathcal{S}}_{0}}\|^{2}\right).

Then with Lemma
9
, under the event
\cup_{l=0}^{t}{\mathcal{E}}_{l}
, we have

{\mathbb{P}}\left(\left|\sum_{l=0}^{t}\prod_{j=l}^{t}\left(1-\frac{C_{a}}{j+C_{b}s\log(2d/s)}\right)\frac{1}{l+C_{b}s\log(2d/s)}\boldsymbol{\varXi}_{1}^{(l)}\right|\geq c_{1}\frac{C^{*}}{C_{a}}\frac{s\log(2d/s)+\delta_{t+1}}{t+1+C_{b}s}\sigma^{2}\right.\\
\left.+c_{1}\frac{(\lambda_{\max,s}^{\textsf{Off}})^{2}}{C_{a}\lambda_{\max}}\|[{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}]_{{\mathcal{S}}^{*}\setminus{\mathcal{S}}_{0}}\|^{2}\right)\leq\exp\left(-c_{2}\frac{C_{b}s\log(2d/s)+t}{C_{a}}\frac{\lambda_{\min}^{2}}{\lambda_{\max}^{2}}\right),

where
c_{1}<0.1
is some sufficiently small constant and it further implies

\displaystyle|\boldsymbol{\mathit{A}}_{4}|\leq c_{1}C^{*}\frac{s\log(2d/s)+\delta_{t}}{t+1+C_{b}s\log(2d/s)}\frac{\sigma^{2}}{\lambda_{\min}}+c_{1}\left(\frac{\lambda_{\max,s}^{\textsf{Off}}}{\lambda_{\min}}\right)^{2}\|[{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}]_{{\mathcal{S}}\setminus{\mathcal{S}}_{0}}\|^{2}.

Regarding term
\boldsymbol{\mathit{A}}_{5}
, we have

\left\|\prod_{j=l}^{t}\left(1-\frac{C_{a}}{j+C_{b}s\log(2d/s)}\right)\frac{1}{l+C_{b}s\log(2d/s)}\boldsymbol{\varXi}_{2}^{(l)}\big|{\mathcal{F}}_{l}\right\|_{\Psi_{1}}\\
\leq C\sqrt{\lambda_{\max}}\sigma\frac{(l+C_{b}s\log(2d/s))^{C_{a}-1}}{(t+1+C_{b}s\log(2d/s))^{C_{a}}}\cdot\|[{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{l}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}]_{{\mathcal{S}}_{0}}\|.

Then, under event
\cup_{l=0}^{t}\{{\mathcal{E}}_{l}\}
and by Lemma
9
, we have

{\mathbb{P}}\left(\left|\sum_{l=0}^{t}\prod_{j=l}^{t}\left(1-\frac{C_{a}}{j+C_{b}s\log(2d/s)}\right)\frac{1}{l+C_{b}s\log(2d/s)}\boldsymbol{\varXi}_{2}^{(l)}\right|\right.\\
\left.\geq c_{1}\frac{C^{*}}{C_{a}}\frac{(s\log(2d/s)+\delta_{t+1})\sigma^{2}}{t+1+C_{b}s\log(2d/s)}+c_{1}\frac{1}{C_{a}}\frac{(\lambda_{\max,s}^{\textsf{Off}})^{2}}{\lambda_{\min}}\|[{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}]_{{\mathcal{S}}^{*}\setminus{\mathcal{S}}_{0}}\|^{2}\right)\\
\leq\exp\left(-c_{2}\min\left\{(s\log(2d/s)+\delta_{t+1})\frac{C^{*}}{C_{a}}\frac{\lambda_{\min}}{\lambda_{\max}},\sqrt{s\log(2d/s)+\delta_{t+1}}\sqrt{C_{b}s\log(2d/s)+t}\frac{\sqrt{C^{*}}}{C_{a}}\frac{\sqrt{\lambda_{\min}}}{\sqrt{\lambda_{\max}}}\right\}\right),

under which, we have,

\displaystyle|\boldsymbol{\mathit{A}}_{5}|\leq c_{1}\frac{C^{*}}{\lambda_{\min}}\frac{s\log(2d/s)+\delta_{t+1}}{t+1+C_{b}s\log(2d/s)}\sigma^{2}+c_{1}\left(\frac{\lambda_{\max,s}^{\textsf{Off}}}{\lambda_{\min}}\right)^{2}\|[{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}]_{{\mathcal{S}}^{*}\setminus{\mathcal{S}}_{0}}\|^{2}.

We then only need to consider the term
\boldsymbol{\mathit{A}}_{6}
, and under
{\mathcal{E}}_{l}^{X}
, it has

\left\|\prod_{j=l}^{t}\left(1-\frac{C_{a}}{j+C_{b}s\log(2d/s)}\right)\frac{1}{(l+C_{b}s\log(2d/s))^{2}}\boldsymbol{\varXi}_{3}^{(l)}\bigg|{\mathcal{F}}_{l},{\mathcal{E}}_{l}^{X}\right\|_{\Psi_{1}}\\
\leq C\frac{(l+C_{b}s\log(2d/s))^{C_{a}-2}}{(t+1+C_{b}s\log(2d/s))^{C_{a}}}(s\log(2d/s)+\delta_{l})\lambda_{\max}\sigma^{2}.

Thus, by Lemma
9
, under
\cup_{l=0}^{t}{\mathcal{E}}_{l}^{X}
, it has

{\mathbb{P}}\left(\left|\prod_{j=l}^{t}\left(1-\frac{C_{a}}{j+C_{b}s\log(2d/s)}\right)\frac{1}{(l+C_{b}s\log(2d/s))^{2}}\boldsymbol{\varXi}_{3}^{(l)}\right|\geq c_{1}C^{*}\frac{\lambda_{\min}}{C_{a}^{2}}\frac{(s\log(2d/s)+\delta_{t+1})\sigma^{2}}{t+1+C_{b}s\log(2d/s)}\right)\\
\leq\exp\left(-c_{2}\left(s\log(2d/s)+\frac{t}{C_{b}}\right)\min\left\{\frac{\lambda_{\min}^{2}}{\lambda_{\max}^{2}}\frac{C_{b}}{C_{a}}\left(\frac{C^{*}}{C_{a}}\right)^{2},\frac{\lambda_{\min}}{\lambda_{\max}}\frac{C^{*}C_{b}}{C_{a}^{2}}\right\}\right),

which implies

\displaystyle|\boldsymbol{\mathit{A}}_{6}|\leq c_{1}C^{*}\frac{1}{\lambda_{\min}}\frac{s\log(2d/s)+\delta_{t+1}}{t+1+C_{b}s\log(2d/s)}\sigma^{2}.

Thus, overall, under the events
\cup_{l=0}^{t}\{{\mathcal{E}}_{l},{\mathcal{E}}_{l}^{X}\}
and by the value of
C_{b}\geq C_{a}
and
C^{*}
, with probability over
1-\exp(-c(C_{b}s\log(2d/s)+t)/C_{a})-\exp(-c(s\log(2d/s)+\delta_{t+1}))
, we have

\displaystyle\|[{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t+1}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}]_{{\mathcal{S}}_{0}}\|^{2}\leq C^{*}\frac{1}{\lambda_{\min}}\frac{s\log(2d/s)+\delta_{t+1}}{t+1+C_{b}s\log(2d/s)}\sigma^{2}+c\frac{(\lambda_{\max,s}^{\textsf{Off}})^{2}}{\lambda_{\min}^{2}}\cdot\|[{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}]_{{\mathcal{S}}^{*}\setminus{\mathcal{S}}_{0}}\|^{2},

which completes the proof.

B.2
Proof of Lemma
2

The vector
{\mathbf{G}}
can be written as the sum of two terms

\displaystyle{\mathbf{G}}(\{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t},{\mathbf{X}}_{t},Y_{t}\}_{t\in{\mathcal{T}}})=\sum_{t\in{\mathcal{T}}}{\mathbf{X}}_{t}{\mathbf{X}}_{t}^{\top}\left({\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\right)-\sum_{t\in{\mathcal{T}}}\xi_{t}\cdot{\mathbf{X}}_{t}.

(19)

We first analyze the second term, which contains the noise. It’s worth noting that
\xi_{t}{\mathbf{X}}_{t}
is martingale difference with respect to
{\mathcal{F}}_{t}
, and it has
\|\xi_{t}{\mathbf{X}}_{t}|{\mathcal{F}}_{t}\|_{\Psi_{1}}\leq\sigma\sqrt{\lambda_{\max}}
. Lemma
15
proves that for any
\delta>0
, with probability over
1-d\exp(-c\min\{\delta,\sqrt{\delta|{\mathcal{T}}|}\})
, we have

\displaystyle\left\|\sum_{t\in{\mathcal{T}}}\xi_{t}{\mathbf{X}}_{t}\right\|_{\infty}\leq C\sigma\sqrt{\lambda_{\max}}\sqrt{\delta|{\mathcal{T}}|}.

(20)

It is also noteworthy that there possibly exists dependence between
{\mathbf{X}}_{t}
and
{\mathcal{S}}_{0}
. By Lemma
12
, for any
{\mathcal{S}}\subseteq[d]
with size
|{\mathcal{S}}|\leq s
, we have

\begin{split}&{\mathbb{P}}\left(\left\|{\mathcal{H}}_{{\mathcal{S}}}\left(\sum_{t\in{\mathcal{T}}}\xi_{t}{\mathbf{X}}_{t}\right)\right\|\geq C\sigma\sqrt{\lambda_{\max}}\sqrt{|{\mathcal{T}}|}\sqrt{s\log(2d/s)+\delta}\right)\\
&~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~\leq\exp\left(-c\min\left\{\delta,\sqrt{|{\mathcal{T}}|/(s\log(2d/s))}\sqrt{\delta}\right\}\right).\end{split}

(21)

Then, it only remains to analyze the first term of (
19
), namely,
\sum_{t\in{\mathcal{T}}}{\mathbf{X}}_{t}{\mathbf{X}}_{t}^{\top}\left({\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\right)
. Specifically, we aim to provide an upper bound for entries located on
{\mathcal{S}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}^{c}\cap{\mathcal{S}}^{*c}
, which represent entries that are not included by
{\mathcal{S}}^{*}
and to provide a lower bound for entries supported on
{\mathcal{S}}^{*}\setminus{\mathcal{S}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}
. Firstly, for
i\in{\mathcal{S}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}^{c}\cap{\mathcal{S}}^{*c}
, we decompose it as follows

[\sum_{t\in{\mathcal{T}}}{\mathbf{X}}_{t}{\mathbf{X}}_{t}^{\top}\left({\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\right)]_{i}=\sum_{t\in{\mathcal{T}}}[{\mathbf{X}}_{t}]_{i}{\mathbf{X}}_{t}^{\top}\left({\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\right)\\
=\underbrace{\sum_{t\in{\mathcal{T}}}{\mathbb{E}}\left\{[{\mathbf{X}}_{t}]_{i}{\mathbf{X}}_{t}^{\top}\left({\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\right)\big|{\mathcal{F}}_{t}\right\}}_{{\mathbf{D}}_{1}}+\underbrace{\sum_{t\in{\mathcal{T}}}\left\{[{\mathbf{X}}_{t}]_{i}{\mathbf{X}}_{t}^{\top}\left({\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\right)-{\mathbb{E}}\left\{[{\mathbf{X}}_{t}]_{i}{\mathbf{X}}_{t}^{\top}\left({\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\right)\big|{\mathcal{F}}_{t}\right\}\right\}}_{{\mathbf{D}}_{2}}.

We first bound term
{\mathbf{D}}_{1}
. Recall the definition of
\lambda_{\max,1}^{\textsf{Off}}
. Under the event
{\mathcal{E}}_{\{{\mathcal{S}}_{{\mathcal{T}}},V_{{\mathcal{T}}},W_{{\mathcal{T}}}\}}
, we have

\left|\sum_{t\in{\mathcal{T}}}{\mathbb{E}}\left\{[{\mathbf{X}}_{t}]_{i}{\mathbf{X}}_{t}^{\top}\left({\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\right)\big|{\mathcal{F}}_{t}\right\}\right|\\
\leq\sum_{t\in{\mathcal{T}}}\left\|{\mathbb{E}}\left\{[{\mathbf{X}}_{t}]_{i}[{\mathbf{X}}_{t}]_{{\mathcal{S}}^{*}\cup{\mathcal{S}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}}\right\}\big|{\mathcal{F}}_{t}\right\|\cdot\|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\|\leq\lambda_{\max,1}^{\textsf{Off}}\sum_{t\in{\mathcal{T}}}\|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\|\leq\lambda_{\max,1}^{\textsf{Off}}\sum_{t\in{\mathcal{T}}}V_{t}.

We then consider term
{\mathbf{D}}_{2}
. It is worth noting that
{\mathbf{D}}_{2}
is sum of
|{\mathcal{T}}|
martingale differences with respect to
\{{\mathcal{F}}_{t}\}
and under event
{\mathcal{E}}_{\{{\mathcal{S}}_{{\mathcal{T}}},D_{\mathcal{T}},U_{\mathcal{T}}\}}
, each single term of
{\mathbf{D}}_{2}
satisfies

\displaystyle\left\|\left\{[{\mathbf{X}}_{t}]_{i}{\mathbf{X}}_{t}^{\top}\left({\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\right)-{\mathbb{E}}\left\{[{\mathbf{X}}_{t}]_{i}{\mathbf{X}}_{t}^{\top}\left({\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\right)\big|{\mathcal{F}}_{t}\right\}\right\}\big|{\mathcal{F}}_{t}\right\|_{\Psi_{1}}\leq\lambda_{\max,1}^{\textsf{Off}}V_{t}.

Thus, by Lemma
9
, we have

{\mathbb{P}}\left(\left|\sum_{t\in{\mathcal{T}}}\left\{[{\mathbf{X}}_{t}]_{i}{\mathbf{X}}_{t}^{\top}\left({\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\right)-{\mathbb{E}}\left\{[{\mathbf{X}}_{t}]_{i}{\mathbf{X}}_{t}^{\top}\left({\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\right)\big|{\mathcal{F}}_{t}\right\}\right\}\right|\geq u\right)\\
\leq\exp\left(-\min\left\{\frac{u^{2}}{(\lambda_{\max,1}^{\textsf{Off}})^{2}\sum_{t\in{\mathcal{T}}}V_{t}^{2}},\frac{u}{2\lambda_{\max,1}^{\textsf{Off}}\max_{t}V_{t}}\right\}\right).

Putting the above two equations together and taking the uniform for
{\mathcal{S}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}^{c}\cap{\mathcal{S}}^{*c}
, we have

{\mathbb{P}}\left(\left\|[\sum_{t\in{\mathcal{T}}}{\mathbf{X}}_{t}{\mathbf{X}}_{t}^{\top}\left({\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\right)]_{{\mathcal{S}}^{*c}\cap{\mathcal{S}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}^{c}}\right\|_{\infty}\geq\lambda_{\max,1}^{\textsf{Off}}\sum_{t\in{\mathcal{T}}}V_{t}+u\right)\\
\leq d\exp\left(-\min\left\{\frac{u^{2}}{(\lambda_{\max,1}^{\textsf{Off}})^{2}\sum_{t\in{\mathcal{T}}}V_{t}^{2}},\frac{u}{2\lambda_{\max,1}^{\textsf{Off}}\max_{t}V_{t}}\right\}\right).

Thus, in all, together with Equation (
20
), we have the entrywise norm for the vector
{\mathbf{G}}(\{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t},{\mathbf{X}}_{t},Y_{t}\}_{t\in{\mathcal{T}}})
,

{\mathbb{P}}\left(\left\|[{\mathbf{G}}(\{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t},{\mathbf{X}}_{t},Y_{t}\}_{t\in{\mathcal{T}}})]_{{\mathcal{S}}^{*c}\cap{\mathcal{S}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}^{c}}\right\|_{\infty}\geq\lambda_{\max,1}^{\textsf{Off}}\sum_{t\in{\mathcal{T}}}V_{t}+u+C\sigma\sqrt{\lambda_{\max}}\sqrt{\delta|{\mathcal{T}}|}\right)\\
\leq d\exp\left(-\min\left\{\frac{u^{2}}{(\lambda_{\max,1}^{\textsf{Off}})^{2}\sum_{t\in{\mathcal{T}}}V_{t}^{2}},\frac{u}{2\lambda_{\max,1}^{\textsf{Off}}\max_{t}V_{t}}\right\}\right)+d\exp(-c\min\{\delta,\sqrt{\delta|{\mathcal{T}}|}\}).

We then consider the lower bound of the vector supported on
{\mathcal{S}}^{*}\setminus{\mathcal{S}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}
. We still decompose it into the conditional expectation term and the martingale difference term,

[\sum_{t\in{\mathcal{T}}}{\mathbf{X}}_{t}{\mathbf{X}}_{t}^{\top}\left({\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\right)]_{{\mathcal{S}}^{*}\setminus{\mathcal{S}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}}=\underbrace{\sum_{t\in{\mathcal{T}}}{\mathbb{E}}\left\{[{\mathbf{X}}_{t}]_{{\mathcal{S}}^{*}\setminus{\mathcal{S}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}}{\mathbf{X}}_{t}^{\top}\left({\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\right)\big|{\mathcal{F}}_{t}\right\}}_{{\mathbf{E}}_{1}}\\
+\underbrace{\sum_{t\in{\mathcal{T}}}\left\{[{\mathbf{X}}_{t}]_{{\mathcal{S}}^{*}\setminus{\mathcal{S}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}}{\mathbf{X}}_{t}^{\top}\left({\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\right)-{\mathbb{E}}\left\{[{\mathbf{X}}_{t}]_{{\mathcal{S}}^{*}\setminus{\mathcal{S}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}}{\mathbf{X}}_{t}^{\top}\left({\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\right)\big|{\mathcal{F}}_{t}\right\}\right\}}_{{\mathbf{E}}_{2}}.

We first consider the term
{\mathbf{E}}_{1}
. Notice that
{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}
is supported on
{\mathcal{S}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}\cup{\mathcal{S}}^{*}
and we further decompose it as follows,

\left\|\sum_{t\in{\mathcal{T}}}{\mathbb{E}}\left\{[{\mathbf{X}}_{t}]_{{\mathcal{S}}^{*}\setminus{\mathcal{S}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}}{\mathbf{X}}_{t}^{\top}\left({\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\right)\big|{\mathcal{F}}_{t}\right\}\right\|=\left\|\sum_{t\in{\mathcal{T}}}{\mathbb{E}}\left\{[{\mathbf{X}}_{t}]_{{\mathcal{S}}^{*}\setminus{\mathcal{S}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}}[{\mathbf{X}}_{t}]_{{\mathcal{S}}^{*}\cup{\mathcal{S}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}}^{\top}\big|{\mathcal{F}}_{t}\right\}\left({\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\right)\right\|\\
\geq\left\|\sum_{t\in{\mathcal{T}}}{\mathbb{E}}\left\{[{\mathbf{X}}_{t}]_{{\mathcal{S}}^{*}\setminus{\mathcal{S}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}}[{\mathbf{X}}_{t}]_{{\mathcal{S}}^{*}\setminus{\mathcal{S}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}}^{\top}\big|{\mathcal{F}}_{t}\right\}[{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}]_{{\mathcal{S}}^{*}\setminus{\mathcal{S}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}}\right\|-\left\|\sum_{t\in{\mathcal{T}}}{\mathbb{E}}\left\{[{\mathbf{X}}_{t}]_{{\mathcal{S}}^{*}\setminus{\mathcal{S}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}}[{\mathbf{X}}_{t}]_{{\mathcal{S}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}}^{\top}\big|{\mathcal{F}}_{t}\right\}[{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}]_{{\mathcal{S}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}}\right\|.

By Assumption
3
and Lemma
14
, we have

\displaystyle\left\|\sum_{t\in{\mathcal{T}}}{\mathbb{E}}\left\{[{\mathbf{X}}_{t}]_{{\mathcal{S}}^{*}\setminus{\mathcal{S}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}}[{\mathbf{X}}_{t}]_{{\mathcal{S}}^{*}\setminus{\mathcal{S}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}}^{\top}\big|{\mathcal{F}}_{t}\right\}[{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}]_{{\mathcal{S}}^{*}\setminus{\mathcal{S}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}}\right\|\geq|{\mathcal{T}}|\cdot\lambda_{\min}\|[{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}]_{{\mathcal{S}}^{*}\setminus{\mathcal{S}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}}\|,

and on the other hand, under the event
{\mathcal{E}}_{\{{\mathcal{S}}_{{\mathcal{T}}},D_{\mathcal{T}},U_{\mathcal{T}}\}}
, we have

\displaystyle\left\|\sum_{t\in{\mathcal{T}}}{\mathbb{E}}\left\{[{\mathbf{X}}_{t}]_{{\mathcal{S}}^{*}\setminus{\mathcal{S}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}}[{\mathbf{X}}_{t}]_{{\mathcal{S}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}}^{\top}\big|{\mathcal{F}}_{t}\right\}[{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}]_{{\mathcal{S}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}}\right\|\leq\lambda_{\max,s}^{\textsf{Off}}\sum_{t\in{\mathcal{T}}}W_{t}.

Thus in all, we have the lower bound for
\|{\mathbf{E}}_{1}\|
,

\displaystyle\|{\mathbf{E}}_{1}\|\geq|{\mathcal{T}}|\cdot\lambda_{\min}\|[{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}]_{{\mathcal{S}}^{*}\setminus{\mathcal{S}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}}\|-\lambda_{\max,s}^{\textsf{Off}}\sum_{t\in{\mathcal{T}}}W_{t}.

Then, we are going to find the upper bound for
{\mathbf{E}}_{2}
’s norm. We notice that

\left\|\left\{[{\mathbf{X}}_{t}]_{{\mathcal{S}}^{*}\setminus{\mathcal{S}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}}{\mathbf{X}}_{t}^{\top}\left({\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\right)-{\mathbb{E}}\left\{[{\mathbf{X}}_{t}]_{{\mathcal{S}}^{*}\setminus{\mathcal{S}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}}{\mathbf{X}}_{t}^{\top}\left({\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\right)\big|{\mathcal{F}}_{t}\right\}\right\}\big|{\mathcal{F}}_{t}\right\|_{\Psi_{1}}\\
\leq\lambda_{\max,s}^{\textsf{Off}}W_{t}+\lambda_{\max}\|[{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}]_{{\mathcal{S}}^{*}\setminus{\mathcal{S}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}}\|.

Hence, by Lemma
11
, we have

{\mathbb{P}}\left(\left\|\sum_{t\in{\mathcal{T}}}\left\{[{\mathbf{X}}_{t}]_{{\mathcal{S}}^{*}\setminus{\mathcal{S}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}}{\mathbf{X}}_{t}^{\top}\left({\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\right)-{\mathbb{E}}\left\{[{\mathbf{X}}_{t}]_{{\mathcal{S}}^{*}\setminus{\mathcal{S}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}}{\mathbf{X}}_{t}^{\top}\left({\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\right)\big|{\mathcal{F}}_{t}\right\}\right\}\right\|\geq 2u\right)\leq\exp\left(Cs\right)\\
\times\exp\left(-\min\left\{\frac{u^{2}}{(\lambda_{\max,s}^{\textsf{Off}})^{2}\sum_{t\in{\mathcal{T}}}W_{t}^{2}+|{\mathcal{T}}|\lambda_{\max}^{2}\|[{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}]_{{\mathcal{S}}^{*}\setminus{\mathcal{S}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}}\|^{2}},\frac{u}{\lambda_{\max,s}^{\textsf{Off}}\max_{t\in{\mathcal{T}}}W_{t}+\lambda_{\max}\|[{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}]_{{\mathcal{S}}^{*}\setminus{\mathcal{S}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}}\|}\right\}\right).

Thus, combining these inequalities, we have

{\mathbb{P}}\left(\left\|[\sum_{t\in{\mathcal{T}}}{\mathbf{X}}_{t}{\mathbf{X}}_{t}^{\top}\left({\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}\right)]_{{\mathcal{S}}^{*}\setminus{\mathcal{S}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}}\right\|\geq|{\mathcal{T}}|\cdot\lambda_{\min}\|[{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}]_{{\mathcal{S}}^{*}\setminus{\mathcal{S}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}}\|-\lambda_{\max,s}^{\textsf{Off}}\sum_{t\in{\mathcal{T}}}W_{t}-u\right)\geq 1-\\
\exp\left(Cs-\min\left\{\frac{u^{2}}{(\lambda_{\max,s}^{\textsf{Off}})^{2}\sum_{t\in{\mathcal{T}}}W_{t}^{2}+|{\mathcal{T}}|\lambda_{\max}^{2}\|[{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}]_{{\mathcal{S}}^{*}\setminus{\mathcal{S}}_{\mathcal{T}}}\|^{2}},\frac{u}{\lambda_{\max,s}^{\textsf{Off}}\max_{t\in{\mathcal{T}}}W_{t}+\lambda_{\max}\|[{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}]_{{\mathcal{S}}^{*}\setminus{\mathcal{S}}_{\mathcal{T}}}\|}\right\}\right).

Together with Equation
21
, we have

{\mathbb{P}}\left(\left\|[{\mathbf{G}}(\{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t},{\mathbf{X}}_{t},Y_{t}\}_{t\in{\mathcal{T}}})]_{{\mathcal{S}}^{*}\setminus{\mathcal{S}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}}\right\|\geq|{\mathcal{T}}|\lambda_{\min}\|[{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}]_{{\mathcal{S}}^{*}\setminus{\mathcal{S}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}}\|-\lambda_{\max,s}^{\textsf{Off}}\sum_{t\in{\mathcal{T}}}W_{t}\right.\\
\left.-u-C\sigma\sqrt{\lambda_{\max}}\sqrt{|{\mathcal{T}}|}\sqrt{|{\mathcal{S}}^{*}\setminus{\mathcal{S}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}|\log(2d/|{\mathcal{S}}^{*}\setminus{\mathcal{S}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}|)+\delta}\right)\geq 1-\exp(Cs)\\
\times\exp\left(-\min\left\{\frac{u^{2}}{(\lambda_{\max,s}^{\textsf{Off}})^{2}\sum_{t\in{\mathcal{T}}}W_{t}^{2}+|{\mathcal{T}}|\lambda_{\max}^{2}\|[{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}]_{{\mathcal{S}}^{*}\setminus{\mathcal{S}}_{\mathcal{T}}}\|^{2}},\frac{u}{\lambda_{\max,s}^{\textsf{Off}}\max_{t\in{\mathcal{T}}}W_{t}+\lambda_{\max}\|[{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{*}]_{{\mathcal{S}}^{*}\setminus{\mathcal{S}}_{\mathcal{T}}}\|}\right\}\right)\\
-\exp\left(-c\min\left\{\delta,\sqrt{\delta}\sqrt{|{\mathcal{T}}|/(|{\mathcal{S}}^{*}\setminus{\mathcal{S}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}|\log(2d/|{\mathcal{S}}^{*}\setminus{\mathcal{S}}_{{\mathcal{T}}}|))}\right\}\right),

which completes the proof.

B.3
Proof of Proposition
1

We prove Proposition
1
by induction. Lemma
1
proves the iterates’ dynamics of
t\in[0,\tau_{1}-1]
. Here, we only prove the iterates in the interval
[\tau_{1},\tau_{2}-1]
and for general
[\tau_{l},\tau_{l}-1]
, it can be proved in the same way only with more notations. During
t\in[\tau_{1},\tau_{2}-1]
, the update is

{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t+1}={\mathcal{H}}_{{\mathcal{S}}_{1}}({\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t}-\eta_{t}\cdot{\mathbf{g}}_{t}),\quad t\in[\tau_{1},\tau_{2}-1].

During this procedure, we have
