{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{i}^{(t+1)}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{i}^{*}=\underbrace{\prod_{s=1}^{t}\left({\mathbf{I}}-\eta_{s}\cdot{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{i,s}^{*}\right)\left({\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{i}^{(0)}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{i}^{*}\right)}_{{\mathbf{B}}_{1}}+\underbrace{\sum_{s=0}^{t}\prod_{l=s+1}^{t}\left({\mathbf{I}}-\eta_{l}\cdot{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{i,l}^{*}\right)\eta_{s}\cdot\xi_{s}\cdot{\mathbf{X}}_{s}\cdot{\mathbb{I}}\{a_{s}=i\}}_{{\mathbf{B}}_{2}}\\
-\underbrace{\sum_{s=0}^{t}\prod_{l=s+1}^{t}\left({\mathbf{I}}-\eta_{l}\cdot{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{i,l}^{*}\right)\eta_{s}\cdot\left({\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{i,s}-{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{i,s}^{*}\right)\left({\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{i}^{(s)}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{i}^{*}\right)}_{{\mathbf{B}}_{3}}\\
-\underbrace{\sum_{s=0}^{t}\prod_{l=s+1}^{t}\left({\mathbf{I}}-\eta_{l}\cdot{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{i,l}^{*}\right)\eta_{s}\cdot\left({\mathbb{I}}\{a_{s}=i\}\cdot{\mathbf{X}}_{s}{\mathbf{X}}_{s}^{\top}-{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{i,s}\right)\left({\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{i}^{(s)}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{i}^{*}\right)}_{{\mathbf{B}}_{4}}

It is worth noting that for
s=0,1,2,\ldots,t
, the matrices
{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{i,s}^{*}
may not have the common eigenvectors. In what follows, we analyze each of the term
{\mathbf{B}}_{1},\ldots,{\mathbf{B}}_{4}
for
i\in{\mathcal{A}}
, respectively.

Bound of
{\mathbf{B}}_{1}
. First consider the products of the matrices,

\displaystyle 0\preceq\prod_{s=1}^{t}\left({\mathbf{I}}-\eta_{s}\cdot{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{i,s}^{*}\right),

and under the event
\{{\mathcal{E}}_{t}\}
, we have

\displaystyle\left\|\prod_{s=1}^{t}\left({\mathbf{I}}-\eta_{s}\cdot{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{i,s}^{*}\right)\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\exp\left(\sum_{s=1}^{t}\log\left(\left\|{\mathbf{I}}-\eta_{s}\cdot{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{i,s}^{*}\right\|\right)\right)\leq\exp\left(-\sum_{s=1}^{t}\eta_{s}\cdot\lambda_{\min}({\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{i,s}^{*})\right)

\displaystyle\leq\exp\left(-\sum_{s=1}^{t_{1}}\frac{C_{a}}{s+C_{b}d}\cdot\frac{\pi_{s}}{2K}-\sum_{s=t_{1}+1}^{t}\frac{C_{a}}{s+C_{b}d}\frac{1}{K}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\exp\left(-\frac{C_{a}}{4K}\ln\left(\frac{t_{1}+C_{b}d}{C_{b}d}\right)-\frac{C_{a}}{K}\ln\left(\frac{t+C_{b}d}{t_{1}+C_{b}d}\right)\right),

where the first line uses Equation
23
and the last line is the integral bound for the sequence. The above equation implies an upper bound for
\|{\mathbf{B}}_{1}\|
,

\displaystyle\left\|{\mathbf{B}}_{1}\right\|\leq\left(\frac{C_{b}d}{t+C_{b}d}\right)^{C_{a}/4K}\|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{i}^{(0)}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{i}^{*}\|,

which suggests
{\mathbf{B}}_{1}=O\left(\frac{1}{t}\right)
due to
C_{a}\geq 4K
.

Analysis of
{\mathbf{B}}_{2}
. Notice that
{\mathbf{B}}_{2}
is the sum of
t+1
martingale difference. We are going to prove
\sqrt{t}{\mathbf{B}}_{2}
converges to a multivariate Gaussian in distribution. To do so, we use Cramer-Wold Theorem
[
16
]
and martingale central limit theorem
[
10
,
22
]
. For any
d
dimensional vector
{\mathbf{v}}=(v_{1},\ldots,v_{d})
, we are going to prove
\sqrt{t}{\mathbf{v}}^{\top}{\mathbf{B}}_{2}
is asymptotically Gaussian distributed. Proposition
5
verifies that
{\mathbf{v}}^{\top}{\mathbf{B}}_{2}
satisfies the Lindeberg’s condition. We only need to prove the convergence of conditional variance. Specifically, due to
\xi_{s}\perp{\mathbf{X}}_{s}|{\mathcal{F}}_{s}
, we have

{\mathbb{E}}\left\{\left({\mathbf{v}}^{\top}\prod_{l=s+1}^{t}\left({\mathbf{I}}-\eta_{l}\cdot{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{i,l}^{*}\right)\eta_{s}\cdot\xi_{s}\cdot{\mathbf{X}}_{s}\cdot{\mathbb{I}}\{a_{s}=i\}\right)^{2}\bigg|{\mathcal{F}}_{s}\right\}\\
=\eta_{s}^{2}\sigma_{s}^{2}{\mathbf{v}}^{\top}\prod_{l=s+1}^{t}\left({\mathbf{I}}-\eta_{l}\cdot{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{i,l}^{*}\right){\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{i,s}\prod_{l=s+1}^{t}\left({\mathbf{I}}-\eta_{l}\cdot{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{i,l}^{*}\right){\mathbf{v}},

where
\sigma_{s}^{2}:={\mathbb{E}}\{\xi_{s}^{2}|{\mathcal{F}}_{s}\}.
We then consider the sum of the conditional variance and we are going to find its convergence in probability

V_{{\mathbf{B}}2}:=\sum_{s=0}^{t}{\mathbb{E}}\left\{\left({\mathbf{v}}^{\top}\prod_{l=s+1}^{t}\left({\mathbf{I}}-\eta_{l}\cdot{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{i,l}^{*}\right)\eta_{s}\cdot\xi_{s}\cdot{\mathbf{X}}_{s}\cdot{\mathbb{I}}\{a_{s}=i\}\right)^{2}\bigg|{\mathcal{F}}_{s}\right\}\\
=\sum_{s=0}^{t}\eta_{s}^{2}\sigma_{s}^{2}{\mathbf{v}}^{\top}\prod_{l=s+1}^{t}\left({\mathbf{I}}-\eta_{l}\cdot{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{i,l}^{*}\right){\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{i,s}\prod_{l=s+1}^{t}\left({\mathbf{I}}-\eta_{l}\cdot{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{i,l}^{*}\right){\mathbf{v}}.

We decompose the variance
V_{{\mathbf{B}}_{2}}
into two terms,

V_{{\mathbf{B}}2}=\sum_{s=0}^{t}\eta_{s}^{2}\sigma_{s}^{2}{\mathbf{v}}^{\top}\prod_{l=s+1}^{t}\left({\mathbf{I}}-\eta_{l}\cdot{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{i,l}^{*}\right){\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{i,s}^{*}\prod_{l=s+1}^{t}\left({\mathbf{I}}-\eta_{l}\cdot{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{i,l}^{*}\right){\mathbf{v}}\\
+\sum_{s=0}^{t}\eta_{s}^{2}\sigma_{s}^{2}{\mathbf{v}}^{\top}\prod_{l=s+1}^{t}\left({\mathbf{I}}-\eta_{l}\cdot{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{i,l}^{*}\right)\left({\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{i,s}-{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{i,s}^{*}\right)\prod_{l=s+1}^{t}\left({\mathbf{I}}-\eta_{l}\cdot{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{i,l}^{*}\right){\mathbf{v}}.

We first prove that the second term of the above equation goes to
o(1/t)
. Specifically, we have the following upper bound,

\left|\sum_{s=0}^{t}\eta_{s}^{2}\sigma_{s}^{2}{\mathbf{v}}^{\top}\prod_{l=s+1}^{t}\left({\mathbf{I}}-\eta_{l}\cdot{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{i,l}^{*}\right)\left({\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{i,s}-{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{i,s}^{*}\right)\prod_{l=s+1}^{t}\left({\mathbf{I}}-\eta_{l}\cdot{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{i,l}^{*}\right){\mathbf{v}}\right|\\
\leq\sigma^{2}\|{\mathbf{v}}\|^{2}\sum_{s=0}^{t}\left(\frac{C_{a}}{s+C_{b}d}\right)^{2}\left(\frac{s+C_{b}d}{t+C_{b}d}\right)^{2C_{a}/3K}\left\|{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{i,s}-{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{i,s}^{*}\right\|.

(27)

We first bound
{\mathbb{E}}\left\|{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{i,s}-{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{i,s}^{*}\right\|
using Proposition
4
, namely

\displaystyle~~~~{\mathbb{E}}\left\|{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{i,s}-{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{i,s}^{*}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq{\mathbb{E}}\left\|{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{i,s}-{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{i,s}^{*}\right\|\cdot{\mathbb{I}}\left\{\max_{j}\|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{j}^{(s)}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{j}^{*}\|\leq h\right\}+{\mathbb{E}}\left\|{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{i,s}-{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{i,s}^{*}\right\|\cdot{\mathbb{I}}\left\{\max_{j}\|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{j}^{(s)}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{j}^{*}\|\geq h\right\}

\displaystyle\leq\kappa_{0}{\mathbb{E}}\max_{j\in{\mathcal{A}}}\|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{j}^{(s)}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{i}^{*}\|+\lambda_{\max}\frac{{\mathbb{E}}\max_{j\in[K]}\|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{j}^{(s)}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{j}^{*}\|}{h}\leq\frac{C}{\sqrt{s}}

Hence, as
t\to+\infty
, the expectation of right hand side for Equation
27
is
O(t^{-3/2})
. Thus we finish showing the second term of
V_{{\mathbf{B}}_{2}}
is
O(t^{-3/2})
. We then analyze the first term of
V_{{\mathbf{B}}_{2}}
and prove its convergence under
\lim_{t\to+\infty}\pi_{t}=\pi^{*}\in[0,1]
and under in probability convergence of
\lim_{t\to+\infty}\sigma_{t}^{2}=\sigma_{*}^{2}
.

\left|\eta_{s}^{2}\sigma_{s}^{2}{\mathbf{v}}^{\top}\prod_{l=s+1}^{t}\left({\mathbf{I}}-\eta_{l}\cdot{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{i,l}^{*}\right){\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{i,s}^{*}\prod_{l=s+1}^{t}\left({\mathbf{I}}-\eta_{l}\cdot{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{i,l}^{*}\right){\mathbf{v}}\right.\\
\left.-\eta_{s}^{2}\sigma_{*}^{2}{\mathbf{v}}^{\top}\prod_{l=s+1}^{t}\left({\mathbf{I}}-\eta_{l}\cdot{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{i}(\pi^{*})\right){\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{i}(\pi^{*})\prod_{l=s+1}^{t}\left({\mathbf{I}}-\eta_{l}\cdot{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{i}(\pi^{*})\right){\mathbf{v}}\right|\\
\leq\eta_{s}^{2}\|{\mathbf{v}}\|^{2}|\sigma_{s}^{2}-\sigma_{*}^{2}|\lambda_{\max}\left(\frac{s+C_{b}d}{t+C_{b}d}\right)^{2C_{a}/3K}+2(t-s)\eta_{s}^{3}\|{\mathbf{v}}\|^{2}\lambda_{\max}\left(\frac{s+C_{b}d}{t+C_{b}d}\right)^{2C_{a}/3K}\left\|{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{i}(\pi^{*})-{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{i,s}^{*}\right\|\\
+\eta_{s}^{2}\sigma^{2}\|{\mathbf{v}}\|^{2}\lambda_{\max}\left(\frac{s+C_{b}d}{t+C_{b}d}\right)^{2C_{a}/3K}\left\|{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{i}(\pi^{*})-{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{i,s}^{*}\right\|.

For any
\varepsilon>0
, Assumption
6
guarantees there exists
t_{\varepsilon}
such that for all
s\geq t_{\varepsilon}
,

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}|\sigma_{s}^{2}-\sigma_{*}^{2}|\leq\varepsilon,\quad{\mathbb{E}}\left\|{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{i}(\pi^{*})-{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{i,s}^{*}\right\|\leq\varepsilon.

Thus, summing up the above equations and take expectation outsize, for all sufficiently large
t
, we have

{\mathbb{E}}\left|\sum_{s=0}^{t}\eta_{s}^{2}\sigma_{s}^{2}{\mathbf{v}}^{\top}\prod_{l=s+1}^{t}\left({\mathbf{I}}-\eta_{l}\cdot{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{i,l}^{*}\right){\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{i,s}^{*}\prod_{l=s+1}^{t}\left({\mathbf{I}}-\eta_{l}\cdot{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{i,l}^{*}\right){\mathbf{v}}\right.\\
\left.-\sum_{s=0}^{t}\eta_{s}^{2}\sigma_{*}^{2}{\mathbf{v}}^{\top}\prod_{l=s+1}^{t}\left({\mathbf{I}}-\eta_{l}\cdot{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{i}(\pi^{*})\right){\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{i}(\pi^{*})\prod_{l=s+1}^{t}\left({\mathbf{I}}-\eta_{l}\cdot{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{i}(\pi^{*})\right){\mathbf{v}}\right|\leq C\frac{\varepsilon}{t},

which implies

\lim_{t\to+\infty}t{\mathbb{E}}\left|\sum_{s=0}^{t}\eta_{s}^{2}\sigma_{s}^{2}{\mathbf{v}}^{\top}\prod_{l=s+1}^{t}\left({\mathbf{I}}-\eta_{l}\cdot{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{i,l}^{*}\right){\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{i,s}^{*}\prod_{l=s+1}^{t}\left({\mathbf{I}}-\eta_{l}\cdot{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{i,l}^{*}\right){\mathbf{v}}\right.\\
\left.-\sum_{s=0}^{t}\eta_{s}^{2}\sigma_{*}^{2}{\mathbf{v}}^{\top}\prod_{l=s+1}^{t}\left({\mathbf{I}}-\eta_{l}\cdot{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{i}(\pi^{*})\right){\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{i}(\pi^{*})\prod_{l=s+1}^{t}\left({\mathbf{I}}-\eta_{l}\cdot{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{i}(\pi^{*})\right){\mathbf{v}}\right|=0.

Thus, we have convergence in probability,

\lim_{t\to+\infty}t\sum_{s=0}^{t}\eta_{s}^{2}\sigma_{s}^{2}{\mathbf{v}}^{\top}\prod_{l=s+1}^{t}\left({\mathbf{I}}-\eta_{l}\cdot{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{i,l}^{*}\right){\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{i,s}^{*}\prod_{l=s+1}^{t}\left({\mathbf{I}}-\eta_{l}\cdot{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{i,l}^{*}\right){\mathbf{v}}\\
=\lim_{t\to+\infty}t\sum_{s=0}^{t}\eta_{s}^{2}\sigma_{*}^{2}{\mathbf{v}}^{\top}\prod_{l=s+1}^{t}\left({\mathbf{I}}-\eta_{l}\cdot{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{i}(\pi^{*})\right){\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{i}(\pi^{*})\prod_{l=s+1}^{t}\left({\mathbf{I}}-\eta_{l}\cdot{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{i}(\pi^{*})\right){\mathbf{v}}.

(28)

Hence, in order to have
\lim_{t\to+\infty}tV_{{\mathbf{B}}_{2}}
, we only need to study the right hand side of Equation
28
. Notice that
{\mathbf{I}}-\eta_{l}\cdot{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{i}(\pi^{*})
and
{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{i}(\pi^{*})
have the common eigenvectors, thus they can be diagonalized simultaneously. Recall that
{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{i}(\pi^{*})
has the singular value decomposition
{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{i}(\pi^{*})={\mathbf{U}}_{i}(\pi^{*})\boldsymbol{\Lambda}_{i}(\pi^{*}){\mathbf{U}}_{i}(\pi^{*})^{\top}
, where
{\mathbf{U}}_{i}(\pi^{*})
is an orthogonal matrix and
\boldsymbol{\Lambda}_{i}(\pi^{*})=\operatorname{diag}(\lambda_{1}(\pi^{*}),\ldots,\lambda_{d}(\pi^{*}))
is a diagonal matrix. Then the matrix part for Equation
28
RHS can be rewritten as

{\mathbf{U}}(\pi^{*})^{\top}\sum_{s=0}^{t}\eta_{s}^{2}\prod_{l=s+1}^{t}\left({\mathbf{I}}-\eta_{l}\cdot{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{i}(\pi^{*})\right){\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{i}(\pi^{*})\prod_{l=s+1}^{t}\left({\mathbf{I}}-\eta_{l}\cdot{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{i}(\pi^{*})\right){\mathbf{U}}(\pi^{*})\\
=\left(\begin{matrix}\displaystyle\sum_{s=0}^{t}\eta_{s}^{2}\prod_{l=s+1}^{t}(1-\eta_{l}\lambda_{1}(\pi^{*}))^{2}\lambda_{1}(\pi^{*})&&\\
&\ddots&\\
&&\sum_{s=0}^{t}\eta_{s}^{2}\prod_{l=s+1}^{t}(1-\eta_{l}\lambda_{d}(\pi^{*}))^{2}\lambda_{d}(\pi^{*})\end{matrix}\right).

Equivalently, we only need to analyze the convergence of the diagonal entries. For the simplicity of presentation, we write
\lambda_{j}
representing
\lambda_{j}(\pi^{*})
. To start with, we employ the Taylor’s expansion of function
\log(1-x)
and it arrives at

\displaystyle\sum_{s=0}^{t}\eta_{s}^{2}\prod_{l=s+1}^{t}(1-\eta_{l}\lambda_{j})^{2}\lambda_{j}

\displaystyle=\sum_{s=0}^{t}\eta_{s}^{2}\exp\left(2\sum_{l=s+1}^{t}\log\left(1-\eta_{l}\lambda_{j}\right)\right)\lambda_{j}

\displaystyle=\sum_{s=0}^{t}\eta_{s}^{2}\exp\left(-2\sum_{l=s+1}^{t}\eta_{l}\lambda_{j}+O\left(\sum_{l=s+1}^{t}\eta_{l}^{2}\lambda_{j}^{2}\right)\right)\lambda_{j}.

We then insert the stepsize values into the equation. Specifically, the summation is

\displaystyle\sum_{l=s+1}^{t}\eta_{l}\lambda_{j}

\displaystyle=\frac{C_{a}\lambda_{j}}{\lambda_{\min}}\sum_{l=s+1}^{t}\frac{1}{l+C_{b}d}=\frac{C_{a}\lambda_{j}}{\lambda_{\min}}\sum_{l=s+1}^{t}\frac{1}{l+C_{b}d}

\displaystyle=\frac{C_{a}\lambda_{j}}{\lambda_{\min}}\int_{s+C_{b}d+1}^{t+C_{b}d+1}\frac{1}{x}{\rm d}x+\frac{C_{a}\lambda_{j}}{\lambda_{\min}}O\left(\sum_{l=s+1}^{t}\left(\frac{1}{l+C_{b}d}\right)^{2}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{C_{a}\lambda_{j}}{\lambda_{\min}}\log\left(\frac{t+C_{b}d+1}{s+C_{b}d+1}\right)+\frac{C_{a}\lambda_{j}}{\lambda_{\min}}O\left(\frac{1}{s}\right),

where the second line uses the Taylor’s expansion of
f(\tau)=\int_{a}^{\tau}x^{-1}{\rm d}x
. Moreover, similarly, the square term can be bounded with,

\displaystyle O\left(\sum_{l=s+1}^{t}\eta_{l}^{2}\lambda_{j}^{2}\right)=O\left(\frac{1}{s}\right).

Combining the above two equations, it arrives at

\displaystyle\sum_{s=0}^{t}\eta_{s}^{2}\prod_{l=s+1}^{t}(1-\eta_{l}\lambda_{j})^{2}\lambda_{j}

\displaystyle=\sum_{s=0}^{t}\eta_{s}^{2}\exp\left(-2\frac{C_{a}\lambda_{j}}{\lambda_{\min}}\log\left(\frac{t+C_{b}d+1}{s+C_{b}d+1}\right)+O\left(\frac{1}{s}\right)\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{s=0}^{t}\eta_{s}^{2}\left(\frac{s+C_{b}d+1}{t+C_{b}d+1}\right)^{2C_{a}\lambda_{j}/\lambda_{\min}}\exp\left(O\left(\frac{1}{s}\right)\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{s=0}^{t}\eta_{s}^{2}\left(\frac{s+C_{b}d+1}{t+C_{b}d+1}\right)^{2C_{a}\lambda_{j}/\lambda_{\min}}\left(1+O\left(\frac{1}{s}\right)\right),

where the last equation is due to the Taylor’s expansion of
\exp(x)
at
x=0
. We then bound each of the two terms respectively. The first term can be analyzed through Taylor’s expansion of function
\int_{a}^{\tau}x^{\alpha}{\rm d}x
,

\displaystyle\sum_{s=0}^{t}\eta_{s}^{2}\left(\frac{s+C_{b}d+1}{t+C_{b}d+1}\right)^{2C_{a}\lambda_{j}/\lambda_{\min}}=\frac{C_{a}^{2}}{\lambda_{\min}^{2}}\left(\frac{1}{t+C_{b}d+1}\right)^{2C_{a}\lambda_{j}/\lambda_{\min}}\sum_{s=0}^{t}(s+C_{b}d+1)^{2C_{a}\lambda_{j}/\lambda_{\min}-2}

\displaystyle~~~~~~~~~~~~~~=\frac{C_{a}^{2}}{\lambda_{\min}^{2}}\left(\frac{1}{t+C_{b}d+1}\right)^{2C_{a}\lambda_{j}/\lambda_{\min}}\left(\int_{C_{b}d+1}^{t+C_{b}d+1}x^{2C_{a}\lambda_{j}/\lambda_{\min}-2}{\rm d}x+O\left(t^{2C_{a}\lambda_{j}/\lambda_{\min}-2}\right)\right)

\displaystyle~~~~~~~~~~~~~~=\frac{C_{a}^{2}}{\lambda_{\min}^{2}}\frac{1}{2C_{a}\lambda_{j}/\lambda_{\min}-1}\frac{1}{t+C_{b}d+1}+O\left(\left(\frac{1}{t+C_{b}d+1}\right)^{2}\right).

In a similar fashion, we can bound the
O(1/s)
term and it’s at scale of
O(1/t^{2})
. Thus all over, we have the convergence in probability for Equation
28
and
tV_{{\mathbf{B}}_{2}}
,

\lim_{t\to+\infty}tV_{{\mathbf{B}}_{2}}=\lim_{t\to+\infty}t\sigma_{*}^{2}\sum_{s=0}^{t}\eta_{s}^{2}{\mathbf{v}}^{\top}\prod_{l=s+1}^{t}\left({\mathbf{I}}-\eta_{l}\cdot{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{i}(\pi^{*})\right){\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{i}(\pi^{*})\prod_{l=s+1}^{t}\left({\mathbf{I}}-\eta_{l}\cdot{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{i}(\pi^{*})\right){\mathbf{v}}\\
=v^{\top}{\mathbf{U}}\left(\begin{matrix}\frac{C_{a}\sigma_{*}^{2}}{\lambda_{\min}}\frac{C_{a}\lambda_{1}/\lambda_{\min}}{2C_{a}\lambda_{1}/\lambda_{\min}-1}&&\\
&\ddots&\\
&&\frac{C_{a}\sigma_{*}^{2}}{\lambda_{\min}}\frac{C_{a}\lambda_{d}/\lambda_{\min}}{2C_{a}\lambda_{d}/\lambda_{\min}-1}\end{matrix}\right){\mathbf{U}}^{\top}{\mathbf{v}}.

Thus, by martingale CLT
[
10
]
and the Cramer-Wold Theorem
[
16
]
, we conclude that the term
\sqrt{t}{\mathbf{B}}_{2}
converges in distribution to multidimensional Gaussian with mean zero and covariance defined in Proposition
2
.

Bound of
{\mathbf{B}}_{3}
. We still first consider the product of the matrices. For
t>s>t_{1}
, with similar tricks in Section
C
, we have

\displaystyle\left\|\prod_{l=s+1}^{t}\left({\mathbf{I}}-\eta_{l}\cdot{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{i,l}^{*}\right)\right\|\leq\exp\left(-C_{a}\ln\left(\frac{t+C_{b}d}{s+C_{b}d}\right)\right)=\left(\frac{s+C_{b}d}{t+C_{b}d}\right)^{C_{a}}.

On the other hand, tricks for
t>t_{1}>s
, the matrix product has

\displaystyle\left\|\prod_{l=s+1}^{t}\left({\mathbf{I}}-\eta_{l}\cdot{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{i,l}^{*}\right)\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\exp\left(-\frac{C_{a}}{K}\ln\left(\frac{t_{1}+C_{b}d}{s+C_{b}d}\right)-C_{a}\ln\left(\frac{t+C_{b}d}{t_{1}+C_{b}d}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\leq\left(\frac{s+C_{b}d}{t_{1}+C_{b}d}\right)^{C_{a}/K}\cdot\left(\frac{t_{1}+C_{b}d}{t+C_{b}d}\right)^{C_{a}}\cdot{\mathbf{I}}_{d}.

Then we can bound the norm of
\|{\mathbf{B}}_{3}\|
,

\displaystyle\|{\mathbf{B}}_{3}\|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{s=0}^{t}\frac{\left(s+C_{b}d\right)^{C_{a}/K-1}}{(t+C_{b}d)^{C_{a}/K}}\|{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{i,s}-{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{i,s}^{*}\|\cdot\|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{i}^{(s)}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{i}^{*}\|.

Moreover, Equation
C.5
provides the bound for
\|{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{i,s}-{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{i,s}^{*}\|
, and under assumptions of Proposition
4
, we further have

{\mathbb{E}}\left\{\|{\mathbf{B}}_{3}\|\right\}\leq\sum_{s=0}^{t_{1}}\frac{\left(s+C_{b}d\right)^{C_{a}/K-1}}{(t+C_{b}d)^{C_{a}/K}}\cdot\lambda_{\max}\cdot{\mathbb{E}}\left\{\|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{i}^{(t)}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{i}^{*}\|\right\}\\
+\sum_{s=t_{1}+1}^{t}\frac{\left(s+C_{b}d\right)^{C_{a}/K-1}}{(t+C_{b}d)^{C_{a}/K}}\cdot\kappa_{0}\cdot{\mathbb{E}}\left\{\|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{i}^{(s)}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{i}^{*}\|^{2}\cdot{\mathbb{I}}\left\{\|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{i}^{(s)}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{i}^{*}\|\leq h\right\}\right\}\\
+\sum_{s=t_{1}+1}^{t}\frac{\left(s+C_{b}d\right)^{C_{a}/K-1}}{(t+C_{b}d)^{C_{a}/K}}\cdot\lambda_{\max}\cdot{\mathbb{E}}\left\{\|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{i}^{(s)}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{i}^{*}\|\cdot{\mathbb{I}}\left\{\|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{i}^{(s)}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{i}^{*}\|\geq h\right\}\right\}

which implies
\|{\mathbf{B}}_{3}\|\leq O_{{\mathbb{P}}}(1/t)
.

Bound of
{\mathbf{B}}_{4}
. It’s worth noting that
{\mathbf{B}}_{4}
is the sum of martingale differences. We consider
{\mathbb{E}}\|{\mathbf{B}}_{4}\|^{2}
,

{\mathbb{E}}\left\{\|{\mathbf{B}}_{4}\|^{2}\right\}=\sum_{s=0}^{t}\prod_{l=s+1}^{t}\left\|{\mathbf{I}}-\eta_{l}\cdot{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{i,l}^{*}\right\|^{2}\cdot\eta_{s}^{2}\cdot{\mathbb{E}}\left\|\left({\mathbb{I}}\{a_{s}=i\}\cdot{\mathbf{X}}_{s}{\mathbf{X}}_{s}^{\top}-{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{i,s}\right)\left({\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{i}^{(s)}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{i}^{*}\right)\right\|^{2}\\
\leq\sum_{s=0}^{t}\prod_{l=s+1}^{t}\left\|{\mathbf{I}}-\eta_{l}\cdot{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{i,l}^{*}\right\|^{2}\cdot\eta_{s}^{2}\cdot\lambda_{\max}^{2}{\mathbb{E}}\left\|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{i}^{(s)}-{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{i}^{*}\right\|^{2}.

Hence, by Proposition
4
, Lemma
17
and Lemma
16
, we have

\displaystyle\|{\mathbf{B}}_{4}\|=O_{{\mathbb{P}}}\left(\frac{1}{t}\right).

Thus, all over, under the conditions of Proposition
2
,
\|{\mathbf{B}}_{i}\|=O_{{\mathbb{P}}}(1/t)
for
i=1,3,4
and
\sqrt{t}{\mathbf{B}}_{2}
converges to the multivariate Gaussian in distribution.

∎

C.6
Proof of Lemma
6

First of all, we have

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}\left(\left\|\frac{1}{t}\sum_{l=1}^{t}\left\{{\mathbf{X}}_{l}{\mathbf{X}}_{l}^{\top}-{\mathbb{E}}\left\{{\mathbf{X}}_{l}{\mathbf{X}}_{l}^{\top}|{\mathcal{F}}_{l}\right\}\right\}\right\|_{F}^{2}\geq\varepsilon\right)\leq\frac{1}{t^{2}\varepsilon}\sum_{l=1}^{t}{\mathbb{E}}\left\|{\mathbf{X}}_{l}{\mathbf{X}}_{l}^{\top}-{\mathbb{E}}\left\{{\mathbf{X}}_{l}{\mathbf{X}}_{l}^{\top}|{\mathcal{F}}_{l}\right\}\right\|_{F}^{2},

implying that
\frac{1}{t}\sum_{l=1}^{t}{\mathbf{X}}_{l}{\mathbf{X}}_{l}^{\top}-\frac{1}{t}\sum_{l=1}^{t}{\mathbb{E}}\left\{{\mathbf{X}}_{l}{\mathbf{X}}_{l}^{\top}|{\mathcal{F}}_{l}\right\}
converges to zero in probability. On the other hand, by Assumption
6
, we have
\frac{1}{t}\sum_{l=1}^{t}{\mathbb{E}}\left\{{\mathbf{X}}_{l}{\mathbf{X}}_{l}^{\top}|{\mathcal{F}}_{l}\right\}
converges in probability to
{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{*}
. The other two arguments can be proved similarly.

Appendix D
Technical Lemmas

Lemma 9

(Bernstein’s Inequality for Sub-Exponential Martingales)
.

Let
Z_{1},Z_{2},\ldots,Z_{T}
be a real valued sequence of martingale difference with respect to
{\mathcal{F}}_{0},\ldots,{\mathcal{F}}_{T}
. Suppose
Z_{t}|{\mathcal{F}}_{t-1}
is sub-exponential with Orlicz norm bounded by
\|Z_{t}|{\mathcal{F}}_{t-1}\|_{\Psi_{1}}\leq K_{t}
, a.s. , where
K_{t}
is a constant, then

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}\left(\sum_{t=1}^{T}Z_{t}\geq s\right)\leq\exp\left(-\min\left\{\frac{s^{2}}{C\sum_{t=1}^{T}K_{t}^{2}},\frac{s}{2\max_{t}K_{t}}\right\}\right).

Proof.

By Markov’s inequality, it has

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}\left(\sum_{t=1}^{T}Z_{t}\geq s\right)

\displaystyle\leq\exp(-\lambda s){\mathbb{E}}\left\{\exp\left(\lambda\sum_{t=1}^{T}Z_{t}\right)\right\}

\displaystyle=\exp(-\lambda s){\mathbb{E}}\left\{{\mathbb{E}}\left\{\exp\left(\lambda\sum_{i=1}^{T}Z_{t}\right)\bigg|{\mathcal{F}}_{T-1}\right\}\right\}

\displaystyle=\exp(-\lambda s){\mathbb{E}}\left\{\exp\left(\lambda\sum_{t=1}^{T-1}Z_{t}\right){\mathbb{E}}\left\{\exp\left(\lambda Z_{T}\right)\bigg|{\mathcal{F}}_{T-1}\right\}\right\}.

For
\lambda\leq\frac{1}{\max_{t}\|Z_{t}|{\mathcal{F}}_{t-1}\|_{\Psi_{1}}}
, the conditional expectation has

{\mathbb{E}}\left\{\exp\left(\lambda Z_{T}\right)|{\mathcal{F}}_{T-1}\right\}\leq\exp\left(C\lambda^{2}\|Z_{T}|{\mathcal{F}}_{T-1}\|_{\Psi_{1}}^{2}\right).

Apply the procedure to
X_{T-1},\ldots,X_{1}
respectively and then we have

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}\left(\sum_{t=1}^{T}Z_{t}\geq s\right)\leq\exp\left(-\lambda s+C\lambda^{2}\sum_{t=1}^{T}K_{t}^{2}\right).

We then insert the value choice for
\lambda

\lambda=\min\left\{\frac{s}{2C\sum_{t=1}^{T}K_{t}^{2}},\frac{c}{\max_{t}K_{t}}\right\}

into the tail bound and then we have

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}\left(\sum_{t=1}^{T}Z_{t}\geq s\right)\leq\exp\left(-\min\left\{\frac{s^{2}}{C\sum_{t=1}^{T}K_{t}^{2}},\frac{s}{2\max_{t}K_{t}}\right\}\right).

∎

Lemma 10

(Bernstein Inequality based on Orlicz Norm of Random Vectors)
.

Suppose
{\mathbf{Z}}_{1},{\mathbf{Z}}_{2},\ldots,{\mathbf{Z}}_{T}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d}
are mean zero vectors, namely,
{\mathbb{E}}[{\mathbf{Z}}_{t}]=0
and
\|{\mathbf{Z}}_{t}\|_{\Psi_{1}}
exists. Then we have the following holds for any
v>0
,

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}\left(\left\|\sum_{t=1}^{T}{\mathbf{Z}}_{t}\right\|\geq 2v\right)\leq\exp\left(Cd-\min\left\{\frac{v^{2}}{\sum_{t=1}^{T}\|{\mathbf{Z}}_{t}\|_{\Psi_{1}}^{2}},\frac{v}{\max_{t}\|{\mathbf{Z}}_{t}\|_{\Psi_{1}}}\right\}\right),

where
C
is some constant.

Proof.

We notice that

\displaystyle\left\|\sum_{t=1}^{T}{\mathbf{Z}}_{t}\right\|=\sup_{{\mathbf{u}}\in{\mathbb{S}}^{d-1}}\langle\sum_{t=1}^{T}{\mathbf{Z}}_{t},{\mathbf{u}}\rangle.

We shall bound the Euclidean norm using
\varepsilon
-net. Additionally, for a fixed
{\mathbf{u}}
and for any
v>0
, we have

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}\left(\left|\left\langle\sum_{t=1}^{T}{\mathbf{Z}}_{t},{\mathbf{u}}\right\rangle\right|\geq v\right)

\displaystyle\leq 2\exp\left(-\min\left\{\frac{v^{2}}{\sum_{t=1}^{T}\|\langle{\mathbf{Z}}_{t},{\mathbf{u}}\rangle\|_{\Psi_{1}}^{2}},\frac{v}{\max_{t}\|\langle{\mathbf{Z}}_{t},{\mathbf{u}}\rangle\|_{\Psi_{1}}}\right\}\right)

(29)

\displaystyle\leq 2\exp\left(-\min\left\{\frac{v^{2}}{\sum_{t=1}^{T}\|{\mathbf{Z}}_{t}\|_{\Psi_{1}}^{2}},\frac{v}{\max_{t}\|{\mathbf{Z}}_{t}\|_{\Psi_{1}}}\right\}\right),

(30)

which uses the definition of Orlicz norm for random vectors. Suppose
{\mathcal{N}}
is a
1/2
-net of
{\mathbb{S}}^{d-1}
with cardinality
|{\mathcal{N}}|\leq 5^{d}
and we take a union of Equation
30
on
{\mathcal{N}}
,

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}\left(\max_{{\mathbf{u}}\in{\mathcal{N}}}\left|\left\langle\sum_{t=1}^{T}{\mathbf{Z}}_{t},{\mathbf{u}}\right\rangle\right|\geq v\right)\leq 2|{\mathcal{N}}|\exp\left(-\min\left\{\frac{v^{2}}{\sum_{t=1}^{T}\|{\mathbf{Z}}_{t}\|_{\Psi_{1}}^{2}},\frac{v}{\max_{t}\|{\mathbf{Z}}_{t}\|_{\Psi_{1}}}\right\}\right).

For any
{\mathbf{u}}\in{\mathbb{S}}^{d-1}
, there exists
{\mathbf{x}}\in{\mathcal{N}}
, such that
\|{\mathbf{u}}-{\mathbf{x}}\|\leq 1/2
. Thus, we have

\left\|\sum_{t=1}^{T}{\mathbf{Z}}_{t}\right\|=\sup_{{\mathbf{u}}\in{\mathbb{S}}^{d-1}}\left|\left\langle\sum_{t=1}^{T}{\mathbf{Z}}_{t},{\mathbf{u}}\right\rangle\right|\leq\max_{{\mathbf{u}}\in{\mathcal{N}}}\left|\left\langle\sum_{t=1}^{T}{\mathbf{Z}}_{t},{\mathbf{u}}\right\rangle\right|+\sup_{{\mathbf{u}}:\;\|{\mathbf{u}}\|\leq\varepsilon}\left|\left\langle\sum_{t=1}^{T}{\mathbf{Z}}_{t},{\mathbf{u}}\right\rangle\right|\\
=\max_{{\mathbf{u}}\in{\mathcal{N}}}\left|\left\langle\sum_{t=1}^{T}{\mathbf{Z}}_{t},{\mathbf{u}}\right\rangle\right|+\frac{1}{2}\left\|\sum_{t=1}^{T}{\mathbf{Z}}_{t}\right\|,

which shows that

\displaystyle\left\|\sum_{t=1}^{T}{\mathbf{Z}}_{t}\right\|=\sup_{{\mathbf{u}}\in{\mathbb{S}}^{d-1}}\left|\left\langle\sum_{t=1}^{T}{\mathbf{Z}}_{t},{\mathbf{u}}\right\rangle\right|\leq 2\max_{{\mathbf{u}}\in{\mathcal{N}}}\left|\left\langle\sum_{t=1}^{T}{\mathbf{Z}}_{t},{\mathbf{u}}\right\rangle\right|.

Thus we have

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}\left(\left\|\sum_{t=1}^{T}{\mathbf{Z}}_{t}\right\|\geq 2v\right)\leq 2\times 5^{d}\exp\left(-\min\left\{\frac{v^{2}}{\sum_{t=1}^{T}\|{\mathbf{Z}}_{t}\|_{\Psi_{1}}^{2}},\frac{v}{\max_{t}\|{\mathbf{Z}}_{t}\|_{\Psi_{1}}}\right\}\right),

which completes the proof.
∎

Lemma 11

(Martingale Bernstein Inequality for Vectors)
.

Suppose
{\mathbf{Z}}_{1},{\mathbf{Z}}_{2},\ldots,{\mathbf{Z}}_{T}
are
d
-dimensional martingale difference with respect to
{\mathcal{F}}_{0},{\mathcal{F}}_{1},\ldots,{\mathcal{F}}_{T}
, namely,
{\mathbb{E}}[{\mathbf{Z}}_{t}|{\mathcal{F}}_{t-1}]=0
a.s. and we suppose
\|{\mathbf{Z}}_{t}|{\mathcal{F}}_{t-1}\|_{\Psi_{1}}\leq K_{t}
a.s. for some contant
K_{t}
. Then for any
v>0
, we have

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}\left(\left\|\sum_{t=1}^{T}{\mathbf{Z}}_{t}\right\|\geq 2v\right)\leq\exp\left(Cd-\min\left\{\frac{v^{2}}{\sum_{t=1}^{T}K_{t}^{2}},\frac{v}{\max_{t}K_{t}}\right\}\right),

where
C
is some constant.

Proof.

The proof procedure would be similar to Lemma
10
and additionally, Lemma
9
would be used. Notice that for any fixed
{\mathbf{u}}
and for any
v>0
, Lemma
9
indicates that

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}\left(\left|\left\langle\sum_{t=1}^{T}{\mathbf{Z}}_{t},{\mathbf{u}}\right\rangle\right|\geq v\right)

\displaystyle\leq 2\exp\left(-\min\left\{\frac{v^{2}}{\sum_{t=1}^{T}K_{t}^{2}},\frac{v}{\max_{t}K_{t}}\right\}\right).

Then with a similar routine to Lemma
10
, we have

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}\left(\left\|\sum_{t=1}^{T}{\mathbf{Z}}_{t}\right\|\geq 2v\right)\leq 2\times 5^{d}\exp\left(-\min\left\{\frac{v^{2}}{\sum_{t=1}^{T}K_{t}^{2}},\frac{v}{\max_{t}K_{t}}\right\}\right),

which completes the proof.
∎

Lemma 12
.

Suppose
{\mathbf{Z}}_{1},{\mathbf{Z}}_{2},\ldots,{\mathbf{Z}}_{T}
are
d
-dimensional martingale difference with respect to
{\mathcal{F}}_{0},{\mathcal{F}}_{1},\\
\ldots,{\mathcal{F}}_{T}
, namely,
{\mathbb{E}}[{\mathbf{Z}}_{t}|{\mathcal{F}}_{t-1}]=0
a.s. and we suppose
\sup_{|{\mathcal{S}}|\leq s,\,{\mathcal{S}}\subseteq[d]}\|{\mathcal{H}}_{{\mathcal{S}}}({\mathbf{Z}}_{t})|{\mathcal{F}}_{t-1}\|_{\Psi_{1}}\leq K_{t}
a.s. for some contant
K_{t}
. Then for any
v>0
, we have

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}\left(\sup_{|{\mathcal{S}}|\leq s,\;{\mathcal{S}}\subseteq[d]}\left\|{\mathcal{H}}_{{\mathcal{S}}}\left(\sum_{t=1}^{T}{\mathbf{Z}}_{t}\right)\right\|\geq 2v\right)\leq\exp\left(Cs\log(ed/s)-\min\left\{\frac{v^{2}}{\sum_{t=1}^{T}K_{t}^{2}},\frac{v}{\max_{t}K_{t}}\right\}\right),

where
C
is some constant.

Proof.

Lemma
11
indicates that for any fixed
{\mathcal{S}}
,

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}\left(\left\|{\mathcal{H}}_{{\mathcal{S}}}\left(\sum_{t=1}^{T}{\mathbf{Z}}_{t}\right)\right\|\geq 2v\right)\leq 2\times 5^{s}\exp\left(-\min\left\{\frac{v^{2}}{\sum_{t=1}^{T}K_{t}^{2}},\frac{v}{\max_{t}K_{t}}\right\}\right).

Then take the union over all
{\mathcal{S}}
, and it leads to

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}\left(\left\|{\mathcal{H}}_{{\mathcal{S}}}\left(\sum_{t=1}^{T}{\mathbf{Z}}_{t}\right)\right\|\geq 2v\right)\leq\binom{d}{s}\times 2\times 5^{s}\exp\left(-\min\left\{\frac{v^{2}}{\sum_{t=1}^{T}K_{t}^{2}},\frac{v}{\max_{t}K_{t}}\right\}\right),

with
\binom{d}{s}\leq(ed/s)^{s}
completing the proof.
∎

Lemma 13
.

Suppose
g_{1},\ldots,g_{n}
are zero-mean sub-Gaussian random variables and the length
n
vector
{\mathbf{g}}=(g_{1},\ldots,g_{n})

s-\Psi_{2}
Orlicz-norm at most
\sigma
, namely
\sup_{{\mathcal{S}}\subseteq[n],\;|{\mathcal{S}}|\leq s}\|[{\mathbf{g}}]_{{\mathcal{S}}}\|_{\Psi_{2}}\leq\sigma
. Denote by
\left(g_{(1)},\ldots,g_{(n)}\right)
be a non-increasing rearrangement of
\left(\left|g_{1}\right|,\ldots,\left|g_{n}\right|\right)
. Then

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\sqrt{\sum_{j=1}^{s}\left(g_{(j)}\right)^{2}}\geq C\sqrt{s\log(en/s)}\sigma+u\sigma\right)\leq\exp(-cu^{2})

for all
t>0
and
s\in\{1,\ldots,p\}
.

Proof.

Based on the assumption, for any fixed set
{\mathcal{S}}\subseteq[n]
and
|{\mathcal{S}}|=s
, we have

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}\left(\sqrt{\sum_{i\in{\mathcal{S}}}(g_{i})^{2}}\geq C\sqrt{s}\sigma+u\right)\leq\exp\left(-\frac{cu^{2}}{\sigma^{2}}\right).

We then take the uniform for all the
s
subset of
[n]
. It’s worth noting that

\displaystyle\#\{{\mathcal{S}}:\;|{\mathcal{S}}|=s,\;{\mathcal{S}}\subseteq[n]\}=\binom{n}{s}\leq\left(\frac{en}{s}\right)^{s}.

Then we have

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}\left(\max_{|{\mathcal{S}}|=s,\;{\mathcal{S}}\subseteq[n]}\sqrt{\sum_{i\in{\mathcal{S}}}(g_{i})^{2}}\geq C\sqrt{s}\sigma+u\right)\leq\left(\frac{en}{s}\right)^{s}\exp\left(-\frac{cu^{2}}{\sigma^{2}}\right).

It implies

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}\left(\max_{|{\mathcal{S}}|=s,\;{\mathcal{S}}\subseteq[n]}\sqrt{\sum_{i\in{\mathcal{S}}}(g_{i})^{2}}\geq C\sqrt{s\log(en/s)}\sigma+u\sigma\right)\leq\exp\left(-cu^{2}\right),

which finishes the proof.
∎

Lemma 14
.

Suppose
{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{1},\ldots,{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{n}
are symmetric positive definite matrices with minimal eigenvalues greater than
\lambda_{\min}>0
, namely,
\lambda_{\min}({\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{i})\geq\lambda_{\min}
. Then we have
\lambda_{\min}(\sum_{i=1}^{n}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{i})\geq n\lambda_{\min}
.

Proof.

It’s worth noting that
\lambda_{\min}(\sum_{i=1}^{n}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{i})=\inf_{{\mathbf{u}}\in{\mathbb{S}}^{d-1}}{\mathbf{u}}^{\top}(\sum_{i=1}^{n}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{i}){\mathbf{u}}
. On the other hand, for any
{\mathbf{u}}\in{\mathbb{S}}^{d-1}
, we have
{\mathbf{u}}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{i}{\mathbf{u}}\geq\lambda_{\min}
. Thus, we have
\inf_{{\mathbf{u}}\in{\mathbb{S}}^{d-1}}{\mathbf{u}}^{\top}(\sum_{i=1}^{n}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{i}){\mathbf{u}}\geq\sum_{i=1}^{n}\inf_{{\mathbf{u}}\in{\mathbb{S}}^{d-1}}{\mathbf{u}}^{\top}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{i}{\mathbf{u}}\geq n\lambda_{\min}
, which completes the proof.
∎

Lemma 15
.

Suppose
{\mathbf{Z}}_{1},{\mathbf{Z}}_{2},\ldots,{\mathbf{Z}}_{T}
are
d
-dimensional martingale difference with respect to
{\mathcal{F}}_{0},{\mathcal{F}}_{1},\ldots,{\mathcal{F}}_{T}
, namely,
{\mathbb{E}}[{\mathbf{Z}}_{t}|{\mathcal{F}}_{t-1}]=0
a.s. and we suppose
\|{\mathbf{Z}}_{t}|{\mathcal{F}}_{t-1}\|_{\Psi_{1}}\leq K_{t}
a.s. for some constant
K_{t}
. Then for any
v>0
, we have

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}\left(\left\|\sum_{t=1}^{T}{\mathbf{Z}}_{t}\right\|_{\infty}\geq 2v\right)\leq d\exp\left(-\min\left\{\frac{v^{2}}{C\sum_{t=1}^{T}K_{t}^{2}},\frac{v}{\max_{t}K_{t}}\right\}\right),

where
C
is some constant.

Proof.

For any
i=1,\ldots,d
, the term
\sum_{t=1}^{T}[{\mathbf{Z}}_{t}]_{i}
is the sum of martingale differences, with
\|[{\mathbf{Z}}_{t}]_{i}\|_{\Psi_{1}}\leq K_{t}
. By Lemma
9
, for any
v
, we have,

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}\left(\left|\sum_{t=1}^{T}[{\mathbf{Z}}_{t}]_{i}\right|\geq 2v\right)\leq\exp\left(-\min\left\{\frac{v^{2}}{C\sum_{t=1}^{T}K_{t}^{2}},\frac{v}{\max_{t}K_{t}}\right\}\right).

Take the uniform for all
i=1,\ldots,d
and it leads to

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}\left(\max_{i=1,\ldots,d}\left|\sum_{t=1}^{T}[{\mathbf{Z}}_{t}]_{i}\right|\geq 2v\right)\leq d\exp\left(-\min\left\{\frac{v^{2}}{C\sum_{t=1}^{T}K_{t}^{2}},\frac{v}{\max_{t}K_{t}}\right\}\right),

which completes the proof.
∎

Lemma 16
.

For any positive values
\alpha,\beta>0
and any positive integers
t\geq s
, we have

\displaystyle\prod_{l=s}^{t}\left(1-\frac{\alpha}{l+\beta}\right)\leq\left(\frac{s+\beta}{t+1+\beta}\right)^{\alpha}.

Proof.

Since
\log(1-x)\leq x
, we have

\displaystyle\prod_{l=s}^{t}\left(1-\frac{\alpha}{l+\beta}\right)=\exp\left(\sum_{l=s}^{t}\log\left(1-\frac{\alpha}{l+\beta}\right)\right)\leq\exp\left(-\sum_{l=s}^{t}\frac{\alpha}{l+\beta}\right).

Moreover, the decreasing function has

\displaystyle\sum_{l=s}^{t}\frac{\alpha}{l+\beta}\geq\int_{s}^{t+1}\frac{\alpha}{x+\beta}{\rm d}x=\alpha\log\left(\frac{t+1+\beta}{s+\beta}\right).

Thus, it arrives at

\displaystyle\prod_{l=s}^{t}\left(1-\frac{\alpha}{l+\beta}\right)\leq\left(\frac{s+\beta}{t+1+\beta}\right)^{\alpha}.

∎

Lemma 17
.

For any positive values
\alpha,\beta>0
and any positive integers
t\geq s
, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{l=s}^{t}(l+\beta)^{\alpha}\leq\frac{1}{\alpha+1}(t+1+\beta)^{\alpha+1}.
